"related_chapter", "content": "[EXCERPT extracted-character range 0:4000]\n# K-SYS-004：取消与完成：业务终止、设备排空和资源回收\n\n取消撤销未来服务意图，完成证明既有访问已经结束。物理资源重用必须汇合逻辑拥有者、执行使用者与输出消费者的结束证据；业务终止、设备完成、输出提交和地址可复用是四个不同的边界。\n\n## 请求终止与对象生命期\n\n请求可能在接收、排队、准备、执行、生成或输出阶段终止。EOS、长度上限、取消、deadline、内部错误和网络断开有不同的业务含义，但都不能直接证明 kernel、DMA、存储读取或响应消费者已经停止访问对象。\n\n请求实例 r 不复用；其执行代次为 I=(r,g)。物理槽 s 的一次分配生命期为 x=(s,e)，e 是槽代次，不能与请求执行代次 g 混用。完整对象描述还包括对象种类、底层存储范围、模型与表示版本。同一底层存储上的别名必须合并保护，或证明各自访问范围确实不重叠；只按张量名字建账不能保护别名写入。\n\n对对象 x 定义：\n\n- L_x：逻辑拥有者集合，例如请求、缓存目录或恢复副本责任方。\n- U_x：已经取得启动资格、尚未证明最后访问结束的使用责任集合。\n- O_x：已经取得输出读取权、尚未结束的消费者集合。\n- B_x：仍有效的长期绑定，例如应用图缓冲的地址资格。\n- Q_x：尚未解决的隔离原因集合。\n\n对象进入物理空闲需要满足\n\nretiring_x ∧ L_x=∅ ∧ U_x=∅ ∧ O_x=∅ ∧ B_x=∅ ∧ Q_x=∅。\n\nretiring 表示不再接纳新的拥有者或长期绑定。已有拥有者可以在释放自己的 L 之前继续登记合法使用；最后一个 L 被撤销、检查空集合与关闭分配生命期必须在同一个串行状态域内完成。这样不会出现“检查为空之后，另一个线程又取得旧对象”的窗口。\n\n集合不是可以由任意通知递减的计数。两个使用者分别持有 uid₁、uid₂；uid₁ 的重复完成只能再次命中 uid₁ 的已结束记录，不能抵消 uid₂。计数可以作为集合的缓存，但不能代替责任身份。\n\n请求业务状态为 LIVE 或 TERMINATED(reason)，执行阶段与它正交。取消后的 SUBMITTED 工作仍然需要接收完成事件。DRAINED 只表示本章登记的物理访问已经结算，不恢复请求的业务资格，也不表示分配器向驱动归还了全部缓存。\n\n## 三种引用分别证明什么\n\n逻辑引用回答“谁仍有权使用或保留这个对象”。A、B 共享前缀时，取消 A 只撤销 A 的持有；B 的拥有权、缓存目录持有和共享下载的其他等待者继续存在。公共权重通常随模型版本存在，不能由单请求取消直接卸载。\n\n执行引用回答“哪些已经取得启动资格的访问仍可能发生”。登记必须先于启动，且覆盖准备任务、kernel、graph replay、DMA、外部库读者和存储读取。主机提交返回通常只说明提交阶段结束；一个 stream 的完成事件也只覆盖其实际排序到的访问，不能代表任意外部传输结束。\n\n输出引用回答“哪些消费者仍需读取结果”。业务取消阻止新业务提交，但无法撤回已经交给发送缓冲或客户端播放队列的内容。ASGI 的 HTTP response body send 成功返回意味着该 body 已交给服务器发送缓冲，不证明客户端已经消费。应用原输出缓冲何时可释放，取决于适配层是复制内容还是继续借用它；不应统一等待远端确认，也不能统一在 send 调用发出时释放。\n\nB_x 记录具体绑定要求。应用维护的固定图输入地址可以要求保留地址资格；CUDA 图内存节点则有 GPU ordered lifetime，固定虚拟地址不意味着物理 backing 必须在全部图节点生命期内独占保留。应按实际分配与图契约建账，不能把所有图对象一律解释成永久物理占用。\n\n## 完成证书、隔离与迟到事件\n\n为每项使用登记票据 τ=(r,g,x,uid,range,kind)。range 是该责任可能访问的底层字节范围，kind 区分准备、计算、传输与其他使用。完成适配器把平台事件转换为证书 κ，说明它覆盖哪张票据、哪些最后访问已经结束、业务结果是否有效。\n\n“访问已经结束”和“结果成功”分别记录。失败操作若提供完整的最后访问结束证据，可以结算自己的 U，但其部分数据仍不能发布。部分范围成功、仅收到通知、提交返回或等待超时，都不足以结算整项责任。本章算法采用保守的整票据结算；需要分段回收时，应在启动前拆出范围互不混淆的票据。\n\n证书处理分三类：已登记且匹配的完成结算自己的责任；已结束票据的重复完成无状态变化；身份、范围或证据不匹配的事件保留原责任并记录诊断。无法归属的旧事件不能反过来清除或隔离当前新对象。\n\nQ_x 必须带原因和解除条件。若隔离原因只是“票据 τ 超时，尚无最后访问证据”，后来 τ 的真实完整完成可以同时移除 U 中的 τ 和对应隔离原因。其他票据、注册区或故障域的隔离不能随之清除。若上下文失效后已经无法取得单项完成，只能依靠平台明确支持的整个访问域停止、映射撤销或重建证据解除相应隔离；不能用第二次超时代替证据。\n\n仅递增 e 或 g 可以拒绝旧结果提交，不能阻止旧 DMA 写入同一物理地址。地址必须保持隔离到最后访问结束，或者通过有明确停止保证的平台路径撤销旧访问权。\n\n## 不同终止阶段的合法动作\n\n| 触发点 | 可以立即改变 | 必须继续保留的责任 |\n|---|---|---|\n| 尚未提交的排队请求 | 撤出队列，撤销业务资格 | 已取得启动资格的解析、分词或读取任务 |\n| 准备中取消 | 禁止后继提交，放弃未启动阶段 | 已启动的快照、页表、转换和预取访问 |\n| GPU 执行中取消 | 禁止后续迭代和新业务输出 | 已提交 kernel、graph 及独立读者的最后访问 |\n| 存储读取或转换失败 | 不发布失败范围，停止其依赖计算 | 仍可能写入 staging 的 I/O 与转换任务 |\n| 传输中取消 | 停止新 WR 或分段，撤销未授权接管 | 已提交部分、目标写入和源恢复副本责任 |\n| 已提交输出后断开 | 禁止新输出，通知相关消费者结束 | 已借用的发送缓冲、编码任务或客户端旧代队列 |\n| worker 或执行组失效 | 撤销路由和后续业务提交 | 外部拥有者、注册区、对端和执行组的清理责任 |\n\n请求取消不能让某个 rank 单独跳过其他 ranks 已经进入的 collective。受影响组需要按相应通信库和[恢复协议](serving-recovery.md)共同结束或重建。请求数归零也不能作为模型卸载完成条件：预取、图、编译、传输和输出仍可能持有版本状态。\n\n## 终止、提交与回收的事件算法\n\n以下是一种保守实现：状态拥有者串行处理事件；多线程实现用等价的锁或原子事务提供线性化顺序。它按整个物理对象回收，不提供任意字节段的并行重分配。事件处理不能在等待设备或满数据队列时占住唯一状态线程，否则完成通知可能永远无法解除责任。\n\n生产者只修改本代私有候选，不直接推进请求历史、RNG 或对外输出。COMMIT 的资格检查和安装属于同一个事务。迁移场景还必须使用下一节的权威提交域：源端预先读取一次授权，随后在本地独立安装结果，不满足这里的原子性要求。\n\n```pseudocode\n算法 请求终止与对象回收\n输入：请求、分配、使用、提交、消费者结束和故障事件\n输出：唯一业务终态、已提交输出序列、空闲对象和具体阻塞责任\n\n初始化：\n  request、object、operation、已结束票据记录为空\n  对每槽 s：current[s]=空，epoch[s]=-1\n  对每对象：L、U、O、B、Q 为空；retiring=false；freed=false\n\n循环取事件，在同一状态域内处理：\n\nARRIVE(r,规范化输入,limit)：\n  若 r 已存在：执行入口去重，不重建其状态\n  验证输入；建立 live=true、active_g=0、输出序号=0、提交记录为空\n  若输入非法：TERMINATE(r,ERROR)\n  否则若 limit=0：TERMINATE(r,LENGTH)\n  否则进入等待队列\n\nALLOCATE(s,r)：\n  要求 r 仍可服务，current[s]=空，旧生命期无未结责任\n  失败：不改变 epoch、current 或任何引用\n  成功：e=epoch[s]+1；建立 x=(s,e)，L_x={r}\n        epoch[s]=e；current[s]=x；记录实际物理容量\n\nATTACH_OWNER(x,o)：\n  要求 x 尚未释放且未 retiring，拥有者具有合法取得资格\n  原子加入 L_x；拒绝事件不生成拥有权\n\nSTART_OPERATION(I,uid,对象与范围集合)：\n  要求请求 LIVE、I 为当前可启动代次、对象身份与快照一致\n  要求启动者持有各对象的 L，且范围、权限、依赖合法\n  先对每个对象登记对应 τ 到 U，并保存 operation 记录\n  再发起实际操作；主机返回不结算已提交的 τ\n  若明确全部未启动：以未启动证据撤销本次登记\n  若部分提交：只撤销已证明未启动的部分\n  若提交结果不明确：保留可能启动的责任及隔离原因\n\nTERMINATE(r,reason)：\n  若已终止：幂等确认，保留第一次确定的业务终态\n  否则在提交状态域内设置 live=false、commit_eligible=false、terminal=reason\n  撤销未取得启动资格的后继任务\n  撤销 r 的逻辑持有；不删除其他拥有者\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 12000:15083]\n让持有时间随并发增长，该占用又会减少准入容量，形成反馈，不能继续把 0.5 秒当常量。迁移门控期间也需同时保留源快照、目标副本及仍在途的私有候选，按实际物理并集计算峰值。\n\n吞吐模型必须区分工作量与墙钟时间。设正常请求消耗 G 个设备秒，取消请求在取消前消耗 fG、之后仍消耗 D 个设备秒，取消比例为 c，设备可提供 P 个设备秒/秒。忽略其他瓶颈时，有用正常完成率满足\n\nX_useful ≤ P(1−c) / [(1−c)G+c(fG+D)]。\n\nD 是剩余设备工作，不能把纯网络等待或空闲隔离墙钟时间直接填入。隔离容量、CPU 锁、权威提交处理和带宽还会给出其他约束，因此该式不是服务吞吐的通用预测。\n\n## 条件变化后的方案选择\n\n| 条件变化 | 必须保留 | 可以调整及其反转信号 |\n|---|---|---|\n| 接口提供真正中止与结束证据 | 证据覆盖最后访问 | 用中止缩短排空；仅调用返回而无结束证明时继续隔离 |\n| 单 stream 改为跨 stream 或外部 DMA | 全部实际使用者均受保护 | 重建 event、注册区和外部完成依赖，不能沿用单队列假设 |\n| 共享前缀或共享下载增加 | 每个拥有者与在途使用独立结算 | 缓存可延迟淘汰；不能把单请求终止变成共享操作取消 |\n| 高取消率与大对象 | 未释放物理容量仍计入准入 | 限制在途深度、缩短可中止工作单位或降低准入；加槽只缓解暂态 |\n| 小对象与高事件率 | uid 幂等及覆盖关系 | 合并票据与批量处理；通知滞后超过收益时需缩小合并窗口 |\n| 图或注册绑定长期存在 | 地址资格与访问责任分别成立 | 拆分生命期、解除不再需要的绑定；不能仅因一次 replay 完成就卸载 |\n| worker 崩溃 | 外部责任和旧提交权可判定 | 按访问域重建、授权代次和对端协议清理；局部引用计数不再充分 |\n| 输出消费者很慢 | 借用缓冲的实际结束边界 | 有界背压、复制后交还或终止消费者；复制减少设备驻留但增加主存与流量 |\n| 提交域与业务状态分离 | 最终安装必须接受 fencing | 引入共同事务或权威提交接收端；单次读取授权方案应放弃 |\n| 最终增量过大或静止等待过长 | 最终 p 覆盖全部已提交状态 | 在合法不可变前缀上预复制、缩小提交单位，或放弃本次迁移；不能放宽快照边界 |\n| 授权服务不可达 | 未知接管结果不能恢复旧提交权 | 等待权威决定或按另有证明的恢复路线终止；不能靠本地超时双端推进 |\n\n## 竞争解释、区分实验与反证修正\n\n| 观察与竞争解释 | 区分实验 | 预测与修正 |\n|---|---|---|\n| 取消很快但槽迟迟不归还：设备排空、通知阻塞、输出借用或泄漏 | 分别延迟设备完成、主机通知处理和输出消费，记录三本账 | 归还随哪个延迟变化就优先查哪条责任；设备早已结束不能继续只归因 GPU |\n| 新请求读到旧数据：提前回收、旧代提交或布局错误 | 分别保持旧存储存活、拒绝旧代提交、固定布局与物理页 | 保活消错支持物理生命期；仅提交门控消错支持结果归属；均无效则重查布局与范围 |\n| 两个读者只有一个完成后就可回收：计数重复递减或 uid 混淆 | 交换结束顺序，重复同一证书，让两个代使用相同局部 uid | 同一票据重复必须无效；旧代同名 uid 不能清除新代使用 |\n| 超时后仍写坏地址：把超时当完成或隔离解除范围过大 | 延迟真实 DMA 完成，对照仅换代次与隔离到结束 | 仅换代次仍出错支持物理访问问题；一个票据完成后其他访问仍应阻止重用 |\n| used 回落但驱动占用不降：缓存池保留或其他绑定 | 停止准入并分记对象 used、池 reserved、图和注册责任 | used 回基线且 reserved 稳定支持合法保留；排空后不可解释增长才支持泄漏方向 |\n| 迁移丢失或重复一个输出：最终快照过旧、旧事务穿越或交付重送 | 分别在最终快照后尝试源提交、在授权预读后暂停旧事务、仅重送已提交 outbox | 前两项应被门控或接收端 fencing 截断；内部日志正确而网络重复时转查交付身份与确认 |\n| 接管确认丢失后双端推进：错误恢复旧授权或 READY 被当作授权 | 丢弃接管成功响应，再让源请求恢复、目标查询决定 | 源条件恢复应失败，目标按 COMMITTED 决定激活；查询不可达应暂停。若源仍可安装，检查是否绕过 A |\n| 迁移暂停长：未结提交、快照写者、增量传输或授权服务 | 分别暂停一张 J 票据、一个 U 写者、网络完成和授权响应 | 各阶段应留下不同阻塞证据；撤销 J 后仍占内存说明物理责任尚在，不能判为提交门控失效 |\n\n正确性数据应让各请求、对象代次与输出序号可区分。无效完成的测试必须先撤销其他会遮蔽错误的拥有者：若 L 仍非空，即使错误地删除 U，释放也可能继续被 L 阻挡，形成没有辨别力的“通过”。\n\n参考回放必须服从动作接受结果。对“旧对象仍在途时尝试重用，重用被拒绝，随后旧对象完成”的轨迹，拒绝的重用不能出现在历史拥有者集合中。否则参考会凭空创造一个新拥有者，把合法释放判成错误。需要同时检查拒绝动作无资源副作用、正常成功轨迹和能触发错误的负对照。\n\n迁移检查还应核对完整已提交前缀，而不只统计某一时刻 owner 的数量。一个系统可以始终只有一个 owner，却仍让目标从过旧快照恢复；也可以正确拒绝新源提交，却遗漏关门前已进入、尚未安装的事务。固定预期边界、内容、幂等身份和提交前驱，才能区分这些错误。\n\n## 压力下的最终守恒检查\n\n反复准入、取消、部分提交失败、重复完成和槽重用后，停止新请求并继续处理解除责任的事件。检查私有拥有权、在途访问和输出借用结束，共享引用恰好等于仍存活的拥有者，旧代次没有业务提交权，借用额度在真实释放后归还。\n\n迁移停止后同时检查：每个尝试有唯一权威决定；放弃尝试的 READY 不可再次接管；成功接管边界等于关门并结算后的完整前缀；没有未结旧提交票据；已撤销票据与旧 term 不能安装；重复激活不回退目标状态。物理排空继续独立核对，不能由这些业务条件替代。\n\n物理容量按唯一分配求和；池的 used 是 reserved 的一部分，不能二者相加。允许保留的权重、缓存和 arena 应有预先声明的基线。未完成对象必须列出具体票据和解除条件；不得把等待超时、请求数为零或进程退出写成全部资源已经回收。\n\n## 配套检查与证据范围\n\n配套文件为 `tools/check_k_sys_004_full.py`，只使用 Python 标准库，确定、离线、只读。资源模型按对象保存责任集合，参考从带固定接受结果的历史重建扁平责任，并逐事件比较完整状态。覆盖共享引用、取消后提交、执行代次、超时、无效或部分范围证书、重复旧完成、旧释放、输出消费者、绑定、读者结束顺序和四类阻塞组合。拒绝的分配不会进入参考历史，也不推进槽代次。\n\n迁移模型把进入、授权预读、安装、撤销、关门、冻结、READY、接管、激活和确认丢失分成事件。独立历史审计检查安装票据与前驱、最终快照的完整前缀、接管前 J=∅、授权变更、放弃尝试失效和重复激活；关键拒绝另有固定预期。负对照实际允许快照后源推进、跳过未结事务、缓存授权后直接安装、遗漏最后提交、接受错误 READY、把 READY 当授权、确认超时回退和忽略提交前", "excerpt_ranges": [[0, 4000], [12000, 15083]], "range_unit": "zero-based half-open characters in extracted text", "extracted_characters": 18279, "selected_characters": 7083, "extraction": "UTF-8 text", "selection_reason": "Ranked 4000-character windows by current entry title/tags/questions and algorithm/mechanism keywords; ties retain source order."}, {"path": "knowledge/system-semantics/design-synthesis.md", "sha256": "8fce90e6b259f26310f6d16a5de40c4435f49d94d47da3831473af9e96376181", "reading_scope": "excerpt", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "[EXCERPT extracted-character range 8000:15083]\natch 需同时计算路径、排队、偏斜与接收端容量。若做 prefill/decode 分离，还需计状态传输、分片转换和完整提交。较小 KV 不等于网络免费，也不意味着专家热点解决。\n\n区分实验可以固定模型和请求，只改变 EP 度数，记录每链路字节、专家最大负载及尾延迟；固定 EP、只改变 TP，记录本地分片与归约时间；固定并行计划、改变路由或共享前缀，观察队列和流量是否按预测变化。训练时的路由均衡目标不保证每次推理严格均衡。若实际干预同时改变副本、路由和 batch，需要拆开或记录这些中介变化，不能用一个平均吞吐裁决所有解释。\n\n## 场景四：静态编译平台上的混合长度任务\n\n模型函数固定而服务形状变化时，可按 prompt、query、context 和 batch 的联合分布选择有限桶。设备保留 KV，控制面推进真实长度；越界走明确回退、较粗桶或拒绝，不能静默截断。编译准备、executable、workspace 和图池驻留共同进入启动与容量预算。\n\n前缀命中改变待算 query 分布，连续批改变 batch 分布，投机改变验证长度分布。桶选择应在这些机制组合后的 workload 上推导。命中后 query 变短、host 固定成本上升时，大 decode 桶未必优于专门小桶；长请求比例上升后，粗桶的 padding 和 KV 驻留又会改变比较。\n\n固定 NxD Inference 文档的前缀缓存路径先 gather 块 KV，再转换到 flat 布局计算，最后 scatter 回去；这说明支持共享状态不等于直接使用 PagedAttention。布局转换的准备、读取、写入与驻留要进入该候选，不能把另一 backend 的流量模型直接移植过来。\n\n## 组合轨迹：CPU 多请求生成的完整推导\n\n以下参数全部是可复算设定，不是目标机器或模型实测。任务是固定版本稠密 GQA decoder 文本生成，质量由独立固定前缀 forward 与任务评估判定。8 个活跃交互请求，平均外部到达 2 请求/s，每请求生成 64 token，平均输入 512 token，缓存计算历史上限 2048。客户端 ITL 目标为 60 ms；主机与响应预算暂设 4 ms，真实排队和网络是否满足仍需测量。\n\n硬件设定为一个 32 核、32 GiB 可用主存服务域，主存读带宽模型参数为 96 GiB/s；权重与已选布局合计 3 GiB，固定和临时工作区预算 2 GiB。状态参数为 24 层、4 个 KV head、head_dim＝128、每元素 2 字节。dtype、累加与指令路径仍须由实现确认，不能由权重大小推断。\n\n### 模型与容量\n\n每 token 全层 KV 为 2×24×4×128×2＝49,152 字节，即 48 KiB。2048 token 历史为每请求 96 MiB；8 请求为 768 MiB，权重＋工作区＋KV 为 5.75 GiB，另需页尾、输入输出、在途与保留余量。64 个同长请求的主项为 11 GiB；这只说明所列项未超容量。同域两副本各复制 3 GiB 权重与 2 GiB 工作区，再加总计 0.75 GiB KV，主项为 10.75 GiB。\n\n加载按 [制品与布局](../frameworks/checkpoint-loading.md) 建立固定参数，避免不必要的完整转换副本。[分配器](memory-allocators.md)管理请求状态；初版不必使用共享前缀或分页，连续池与固定块池也可保持相同模型语义，应比较长短混合下的碎片和增长。已输出 token 与已计算 KV 的边界分别记录。\n\n### 三个执行候选\n\n| 候选 | 推进组织 | 主要收益与代价 |\n|---|---|---|\n| A：逐请求单独执行 | 每次一个请求推进一个 token，再轮转 | 路径简单，权重在请求间重复读取 |\n| B：单实例连续批 | 同轮合并 8 个就绪 decode，prefill 受时间预算约束 | 摊薄权重读取，需要批次身份、状态与公平调度 |\n| C：同域两个副本 | 每实例处理 4 个 decode | 隔离请求，复制权重与工作区，竞争同域带宽 |\n\n在每次调用读取全部 3 GiB 权重、每请求读取全历史 KV、忽略其他流量的设定下，A 一次流量为 3.09375 GiB，带宽下界约 32.23 ms；8 请求轮转一次约 257.81 ms，无法支持各请求 60 ms 的间隔。B 一轮为 3.75 GiB，下界为 39.0625 ms，相应理想吞吐上界为 204.8 输出 token/s。\n\nC 均分同域带宽时，每实例 48 GiB/s，流量 3.375 GiB，下界为 70.3125 ms，超过目标。两个实例各有独立 96 GiB/s 带宽域时，下界则为 35.15625 ms。副本是否共享资源必须成为条件格；独立域的选择反转也不证明真实时间已通过。\n\n64 请求全进一轮时，权重加 KV 为 9 GiB，下界为 93.75 ms。内存容得下与 ITL 达标是两回事；轮转成多个小批仍有等待其他轮次的时间，admission 不能只按内存上限设置。若目标观测与这些下界冲突，先核对权重是否真的每轮从主存读完、历史读取层级和带宽设定，再作排除。\n\n### 将 prefill、主机和质量接入同一方案\n\n对 B，60−4−39.0625＝16.9375 ms 是理想 decode 下界之外的预算差，不保证真实实现仍有这些余量。计算、额外事务和同步可能先消耗它。需要测混合批时间曲面，再按 [分块 prefill](../algorithms/chunked-prefill.md)选择合法块；所有块均不满足时应调整运行点、准入或资源。\n\n平均到达产生 128 输出 token/s，低于上述理想上界，但还要承担每秒 1024 个新输入 token 的 prefill，所以不能据此断言稳定。分词、调度、响应与输出队列有独立成本和容量；慢客户端、取消与迟到完成都要回传到资源责任账。\n\nCPU 线性层比较无打包与预打包、线程数和 M 形态。B 每轮 M 约为 8，同一权重行可服务多请求。沿输出通道分线程可避免最终归约，沿 K 分线程需要部分和、工作区及同步。线程增多同时改变并行度、NUMA、cache 和争用；主存模型不证明线程越多越快。低位权重还需区分同量化制品的执行正确性和相对原模型的任务质量；字节下降后应重算资源账，不能直接乘理论压缩倍数。\n\n### 一次组合状态轨迹\n\n1. 准入计算唯一 KV、权重、工作区及增长、页尾、输出余量。容量预留不证明 ITL；预留池与池内物化页不重复相加。\n2. 选择 8 个就绪 decode，为各请求保留可写尾页与输出槽，建立不可变执行视图，绑定请求生命期、逻辑位置、页映射、结果槽和轮次。共享尾页追加前按所选 COW 方案取得独占写区。\n3. 按视图读取权重与各请求历史；39.0625 ms 仍是条件化下界。没有时间上界或有效观测时，候选只处于开放状态；质量也可能尚未知。\n4. 完成通知核对轮次与代次，确认必要写入和结果读取达到完成边界，再提交 KV 前沿。到达已知前缀末行、确有合法 logits 且仍有生成配额时，才采样一次；输出按请求和序号入队。\n5. 完整 KV 命中却没有末行 logits 时，不得因待算长度为零而永久等待。可以安全重算最后一个已知 token：逐层读取此前历史、在私有暂存计算当前行，不覆盖共享页；也可以使用绑定相同模型与前缀的有效 logits 缓存。已确认输出重放不再次采样。\n6. 取消阻止未来业务提交，但不提前释放设备仍读取的页。读者结束后再降低相应执行引用；迟到通知只结算旧轮次，不写回新视图或下一生命期。\n7. 若取得 80 ms 的保守客户端 ITL 上界，不能因它超过 60 ms 就排除 B，39.0625 到 80 ms 之间仍含满足目标的值。应收紧上界或取得同作用域观测。\n8. 假设某个预先固定的有限回放，其客户端 ITL 最大值测为 55 ms，且计时含主机、排队与交付边界，该证据只证明该回放时间通过。质量 `pass` 才可在这个有限作用域标为 `VERIFIED`；质量 `fail` 排除，`unknown` 保留待验。该 55 ms 不能直接证明总体 p99 或每次未来请求通过。\n\n上述轨迹不包含真实 CPU 性能计数器或 GPU 测量。真实时间受 cache/TLB、事务、线程调度、同步、网络和负载影响，质量需要另行评估。\n\n### 区分实验与反转条件\n\n先用独立固定前缀参考比较完整、分块与增量 forward，再改变 batch 排列、物理槽位、跨页和取消后复用。输出身份与采样状态分别检查；最终字符串相同不足以证明资源回收或取消正确。\n\n性能实验扫描活跃数、历史、prefill 块、线程和副本，记录实际流量、阶段时间、TTFT/ITL、拒绝、超时与队列增长。首次加载和稳态分开，在独立运行或预定区组保留验证。低负载单请求时 A 可能更合适；独立 NUMA 域资源足够时 C 的隔离价值可能占优；长历史主导流量时 batch 的权重摊薄收益下降；离线且放宽 ITL 时可比较更大批与粗 prefill。改变条件后保留状态不变量，重算成本并重新取得适用证据。\n\n## 十二类完整场景如何连成知识体系\n\n设计场景将各章机制放进共同任务边界。以下入口给出条件化候选与资源推导，不表示目标硬件已经完成性能实验。先固定任务函数、质量与服务边界，再选择路径。\n\n| 任务形态 | 完整场景入口 | 需要一起检验的关系 |\n|---|---|---|\n| C01 CPU 多请求生成 | 本章 CPU 场景 | 权重/KV 流量、线程与 prefill 时间片、开放到达和 ITL |\n| C02 单 GPU 交互 | [CUDA 平台](../hardware/cuda-execution.md) | 容量、带宽、内核与主机间隙、准入和输出 |\n| C03 长上下文高复用 | [前缀缓存](../algorithms/prefix-cache-identity.md) | 物理共享容量、逻辑读取、路由与长尾 |\n| C04 显存不足与三级存储 | [多级驻留](../algorithms/tiered-residency.md) | 有效副本、预取、共享带宽、双缓冲与恢复 |\n| C05 CPU–GPU 混合执行 | [异构计算](../algorithms/heterogeneous-execution.md) | 计算放置、传输路径、合并与主机竞争 |\n| C06 多卡 MLA/MoE | [图像扩散](../models/image-diffusion.md)、[视频生成](../models/video-generation.md) | 求解器、CFG、时空切分、VAE 与媒体质量 |\n| C12 非生成推理 | 各自输入、状态与任务指标；五种契约分别验证 |\n\n场景可以交叉：RAG 调用 embedding、检索、重排与生成，多模态服务同时需要多模型放置和低延迟音频。组合时重新计算共同资源与状态，不能把各场景的独占带宽或独立质量结果直接相加。\n\n## 实例方案要能落到服务和平台\n\n多节点任务的执行组可能受 [集群放置](../workloads/cluster-resource-placement.md)拓扑与故障余量影响；共享制品、缓存和提交记录引入 [存储服务](storage-services.md)的相应语义。它们由候选实现还是环境提供，取决于任务；单机和离线引擎不因此自动增加功能要求。[部署环境](deployment-environment.md)说明真实配额、设备和版本，进程存活、文件存在或空闲卡数各自只提供局部信息。\n\n这些示例通过任务条件、状态、计算、流量、容量、交互与观测解释方案。其他设计可以采用不同表示和组织；没有恢复或请求排队的任务不需要补造对应模块。已有优化可以按需求采用、重组或省略，结论取决于实际前提和证据。\n\n## 设计例子的适用范围与未列路径\n\n上述场景不穷尽架构或设计变量。Task 决定交付的函数、质量、性能与资源边界；一个例子的调度器、状态池、阶段和验证顺序不会自动成为另一任务的要求。独立调度、整体编译、持久执行和其他组织方式应按实际结果判断。\n\n机制依赖与研发顺序不同。消费者需要有效输入，不等于工程上必须先冻结全部接口；可以用独立实验检查成本假设，再联合修正模型、表示、融合边界和调度。验证保留实际覆盖范围，最终按任务验收。\n\n候选可以扩展、组合或全部放弃。新方案没有现成章节或来源实现并不失格；改变表示、硬件能力或任务质量后，原先的排除前提也可能不再成立。阅读来源、符合例子步骤或获得状态标签本身不构成正确或更优的证据。\n\n## 布局改变后的完整成本与反转条件\n\n以下是设定反例。原路径在输入已满足布局时计算 1.0 ms，新 kernel 计算 0.6 ms，但需要 0.5 ms 输入转换；两者串行且不能隐藏时，一次执行为 1.1 ms。同一合法转换结果可复用 R 次时，新路径总成本为 0.5＋0.6R ms，原路径为 R ms，严格获益要求 R>1.25；R＝2 时为 1.7 对 2.0 ms。\n\n上游直接产生目标布局、融合转换与计算或改变状态表示，都会改变成本项。输入每次改变则复用前提失效；转换与其他消费者争用带宽也要重算。该公式判断声明的候选，不排除能改变其前提的新设计。准备时间的保守上界超过收益预算时，同样不能冒充实际一定更慢的证据。\n\n## 区分实验、反证与修正\n\n| 观察 | 竞争解释 | 区分干预与预测 | 反证后的修正 |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 命中率上升但队列更长 | 亲和集中；短 query 主机成本；保留页挤占容量 | 控制实际命中边界后改路由；固定命中改 query；固定计算增加容量 | 按真正改变结果的干预修路由、准备或保留策略 |\n| batch 增大后 token/s 上升而 ITL 超标 | 权重摊薄；轮转等待；prefill 干扰 | 固定历史改变 batch，分记每轮与请求等待；单独开关分块 | 收窄 batch、准入或阶段组织，不把总吞吐当 SLO |\n| 两副本没有加速 | 共享带宽；权重复制；路由集中；锁或输出竞争 | 同流量比较共享域与独立域，记录带宽、份额与等待 | 独立域改善才支持带宽解释，否则检查其余路径 |\n| 位宽下降而时间不降 | 解包、转换、归约、fallback；主机准备；事务未降 | 固定形状与质量门槛，分别改变位宽、kernel 与线程，核对实际路径 | 撤回不受观察支持的带宽归因，数值与质量另验 |\n| 图重放仍有主机间隙 | 捕获不完整；缓冲更新；输入等待 | 固定请求改变捕获边界，分记 plan/replay，预填输入 | 准备增长则修准备，仍排队则查准入与供给 |\n| 上界 80 ms 超目标 60 ms | 上界宽松；实际确实超标 | 取得同作用域观测或收紧上界；50 ms 的合法结果能反驳“上界超标即失败” | 保留开放候选，只有有效失败观测或下界才支持排除 |\n| 耗时证据通过但不能认定合格 | 质量失败；质量未", "excerpt_ranges": [[8000, 15083]], "range_unit": "zero-based half-open characters in extracted text", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "[EXCERPT extracted-character range 4000:7083]\nte = S.complete 且 P 可信且 U_earliest 为空\n返回全部集合、理由、证书、C 和 S\n```\n\n`NO_FAILURE_FOUND` 只说明已有效检查的部分没有失败，必须连同 `complete`、四类数量及未检查实例交付。只有 `all_valid_no_failure=true` 才能汇总为本次全部义务有效且未发现失败。`Ω` 为空时单独返回未配置义务；若任务允许空集合，也应由任务契约明确解释，不能默认为成功。\n\n例如只检查一条版本不匹配义务时，结果是 `OracleInvalid` 一条、有效检查零条、`NO_VALID_COMPARISON`。一条有效且为真的义务加一条单位不匹配义务时，有效检查一条、`NO_FAILURE_FOUND`、`complete=false`。两者都不满足全部有效且未发现失败。若局部失败已确认，但它之前的采集缺失，则即使 `D` 为空，汇总仍是 `FAILURE_FOUND`，同时说明最早性未解决。\n\n`D` 可以有多个互不可比元素。两个 rank 或两个独立 stream 的失败没有因果先后时，前沿必须保留两者。它表示当前证据下的因果极小失败集合，不是唯一根因标签；全局时间戳排序不能替代这个集合。\n\n## 最小复现的合法性与推进算法\n\n复现用例写为 `C=(x′,θ′,ε′,σ′,s0′)`。`C′≼C` 表示按已声明规则删除或缩小一部分输入、配置、环境、并发或注入。合法缩减要保留同类失败 `F`、机制关系集合 `P_keep`、目标逻辑身份映射、相容 oracle，以及必要观测覆盖 `K`。\n\n`K` 在初始样例上声明，绑定检查点、作用域、对象代次映射和所需因果过去；之后不能随着候选变得更难观察而悄悄降低。删除无关 verbose trace 可以合法，删除唯一完成检查点则不合法。输入允许被缩小，因此身份保持不要求输入摘要逐字相同；它要求预先声明的目标映射、版本、单位和 oracle 保持相容，不能把另一个请求或另一次失败当作原目标。\n\n将一次候选观察化为四个三值条件：\n\n- `I`：身份与 oracle 相容；\n- `K_ok`：证据覆盖满足固定的 `K`，没有因缩减丢失必要观察；\n- `P_ok`：必须保留的机制关系成立；\n- `F_ok`：存在同类目标失败。\n\n完整接受谓词是 `A = I ∧ K_ok ∧ P_ok ∧ F_ok`。任何一项已知为假，即可确定拒绝，并保留理由；没有已知假、但至少一项未知时，判为 `UNKNOWN`；四项均真才接受。未知不能作为已确定拒绝的替身，也不能在最终复核中被忽略。这里的真假须有对应证据，不能把没有观察到失败直接填成 `F_ok=false`。\n\n```pseudocode\n算法 有预算的失败最小化\n输入：C0、目标失败 F、关系 P_keep、固定覆盖 K、有限单步变换 A(C)、总调用预算 B\n输出：current、按版本保存的尝试账本、极小性状态、当前未知和未试边界、实际预算\n\n要求每个变换确定地产生严格更小的候选：自然数度量 μ(cand) < μ(current)\n在预算内检查 C0；若未形成相容、完整的 F 与 P_keep 证书：\n  返回 INITIAL_UNPROVEN 及原因，不作极小性声明\n\ncurrent=C0；version=0；账本为空\n重复：\n  固定本版本 current 的身份和完整有限候选清单 A(current)\n  active_unknown=空；pending=空；accepted_this_version=false\n\n  按确定次序遍历本版本每个单步变换 t：\n    若预算不足：\n      pending=尚未判断的变换及其候选；结束本轮\n    若本版本 t 已有有效记录：复用记录，不无条件重跑\n    否则：\n      cand=t(current)\n      检查严格缩小；运行或核对候选，消耗预算并保留尝试身份\n      用同一个完整接受谓词判为 ACCEPT、REJECT 或 UNKNOWN\n      记录 (version,current身份,t,cand身份,尝试,观察,判定,理由,预算)\n\n    若判为 UNKNOWN：加入 active_unknown；保留候选而不改变 current\n    若判为 REJECT：保留确定拒绝证书而不改变 current\n    若判为 ACCEPT：\n      current=cand；version=version+1\n      accepted_this_version=true；立即结束本轮并重建候选清单\n\n  若 accepted_this_version：继续下一版本\n\n  最终复核：\n    核对本版本候选清单、账本身份和完整接受谓词一致\n    若发现 ACCEPT 却未推进，或证据彼此矛盾：\n      返回 INCOMPLETE 及记录错误，不进入无进展的重试循环\n    若 active_unknown 或 pending 非空：\n      返回 INCOMPLETE、current、全部历史及本版本未知/未试边界\n    否则所有当前单步变换均有确定 REJECT 证书：\n      返回 ONE_MINIMAL_ADMISSIBLE、current、全部历史及实际预算\n```\n\n最终复核审计同一谓词和已绑定本版本的证书，不把“仍有 `F` 且保持 `P_keep`”当作更弱的第二套接受标准。若另行重跑产生新未知或冲突，需使相应证明失效并返回未完成，或在显式新增预算下重新评估；不能仅因一次重跑未触发便确定拒绝。\n\n`ONE_MINIMAL_ADMISSIBLE` 的量词是：在所声明的单步变换集合与固定合法条件下，每个当前单步变换都已有不能同时满足 `A` 的确定证据。它不保证按输入字节数全局最小，也不证明组合删除不可能成功，更不证明机制唯一。需要缩小连续参数时，应给出有限候选序列或其他终止论证；仅说“继续减小”不足以保证终止。\n\n未知记录按 `(version,current身份,t)` 绑定。旧版本删除某项无法观察，不代表新版本删除同名项仍未知；改变其他条件后必须重新判断。历史未知保留在账本中，但只有最终版本的未知和未试项阻断该版本的完整极小性证明。\n\n两个受控反例说明这些条件为何必要。某候选保持同类失败与并发关系，但删除了固定 `K` 要求的一段覆盖：`F_ok=P_ok=true`、`K_ok=false`，应确定拒绝。最终复核仍按完整谓词拒绝，不要求继续删除同一项，因此不会在主循环与复核之间反复往返。另一候选删除观察保护后，是否仍失败无法判断；它必须记录为当前版本的 `UNKNOWN`。若其余项都无法合法缩减，算法返回 `INCOMPLETE`，列出该候选、观测缺口和预算；不能因未知记录丢失而输出完整证明。\n\n具体机制也限制可删除关系。跨页读取若覆盖字节区间 `[4093,4103)`，截成 `[4093,4096)` 会删除跨页关系。取消竞态若依赖“旧访问仍在途而槽位已重用”，加入同步或删除旧访问都会移除原触发条件。固定 seed 能固定随机输入，不能单独固定调度、发布关系或对象代次。\n\n## 将故障注入绑定到状态边界\n\n故障实验先定义契约、注入边界、恢复点和所需证据，再选择干预。边界可为\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 8000:12000]\n据 | 本地等待超时不证明对端失败，也不恢复旧提交权 |\n| 数值路径改变 | 输入范围、dtype、累加精度、batch 方式和 oracle | 记录逐位差异、误差、argmax、margin 和首个分歧位置 | 任意浮点差异不自动构成任务语义失败 |\n| 滚动更新中失败 | 新旧版本、就绪容量、在途请求和排空 | 核对版本绑定、实际接收能力、回退容量与旧访问结束 | 进程重启不等于恢复完成 |\n\n组合轨迹要按边界还原。例如请求 A 在 stream S0 发出写入对象 `(slot=7,g=4)` 的设备工作，host 调用返回；随后业务取消 A，应用把槽位交给 B 的代次 5；旧设备工作最后才完成。B 内容错误可以由旧写入、B 自身索引错误或比较身份错配解释。仅增加代次能拒绝旧结果提交，却不能阻止旧工作写入同一物理地址。\n\n区分实验应分别保持旧存储到完成、只增加提交身份检查、固定 B 的索引，并记录旧操作最后访问与 B 首次读取。只有存储保活消除错误时，才优先支持提前复用机制；真实 CUDA 可见性和最后访问仍需设备证据。保活还延长旧对象驻留：若旧对象为 `b` 字节、取消到最后访问相隔 `d` 秒，该条轨迹额外保护量为 `bd` 字节秒；多个旧对象在同一时刻重叠时，峰值按唯一物理范围的并集计，不能把平均字节秒直接当峰值容量。\n\n机制归因写为 `M=(条件,不变量,预期观察,可反驳预测,竞争解释)`。支持某个机制需要：候选边界在明确因果链上先于症状；受控干预使观察按预测改变；该干预能区分至少一个竞争解释；观察与缩减没有换成另一个失败。时间接近、相关性和一次成功均不足以满足这些条件。\n\n## 重复、压力与恢复保留什么\n\n在隔离环境内比较故障前、故障中和恢复后，保持相同 offered workload；改变故障概率或持续时间时，不能同时降低到达负载后把结果归因于恢复改进。记录计划工作、实际发起、拒绝、失败、取消、未完成、恢复时间、无效计算，以及其他租户受到的影响。失败样本不能从吞吐分母中静默删除。\n\n反复取消、分片失败和重试后，停止新输入并继续处理完成与清理。检查私有状态、执行引用和输出借用结束，共享池回到声明的空闲基线。编译缓存、arena、公共权重可以合法驻留，但应有明确所有者与基线；排空后逐轮不可解释的增长才直接支持泄漏方向。超时或请求数为零都不能代替最后访问结束证据。\n\n修复验证包括原触发小例、仍保留机制关系的压力轨迹，以及未参与诊断的代表负载。若修复改变重试、路由、同步或缓存策略，继续检查版本身份和输出提交，避免把崩溃变成静默错误。一个故障点通过只支持该路径与条件。\n\n概率性失败要保留试验预算、交错条件、触发与未触发次数。一次未触发通常不能确定 `F_ok=false`；应按预先定义的统计或调度 oracle 判为未知，或明确限定为某条已固定轨迹。独立重复与选择偏差见[性能实验](statistical-experiments.md)。\n\n## 条件化成本\n\n证据成本分为执行、采集、传输、同步、驻留和复现实验。设记录事件集合为 `E_R`，事件大小为 `b_e` 字节、处理工作为 `c_e`，逻辑记录量是 `B_R=Σb_e`，总采集工作约为 `Σc_e`。它们不是墙钟延迟公式：批量写出可以与计算重叠，也可能争用 CPU、锁、内存带宽、磁盘或网络。同步刷盘、栈追踪与新增设备同步会直接改变关键路径或原竞态。\n\n若向中心节点传输 `B_wire` 字节，有效带宽为 `β` 字节/秒，则纯传输下界为 `B_wire/β`；压缩、索引、副本和重试使 `B_wire` 不一定等于逻辑 `B_R`。证据准备还包括输入快照、符号解析、探针初始化和环境重建。环形缓冲大小、导出队列及保留时间分别决定在线峰值和离线驻留，不能只报最终压缩文件大小。\n\n设定每次运行 20000 条事件、每条 256 字节，逻辑量为 5120000 字节，即十进制 5.12 MB；1000 次为 5.12 GB，尚未计索引、副本和临时展开。该算例是预算推导，不是工具实测。\n\n图分析也有计算与访存成本。`V=|E|`、`M=|P|` 时，邻接表占 `O(V+M)`，无环检查和拓扑推进为 `O(V+M)`。固定相关范围后，可沿拓扑顺序传播“存在失败祖先”和“过去存在缺口”标记；复杂的主体映射、范围连接与覆盖查询另计成本。逐候选反向遍历最多为 `O(|Bad|(V+M))`；显式存全部可达关系可能占 `O(V²)`。每条路径都单独输出时，证书展开量本身也可能很大，不能用一次图遍历的复杂度掩盖输出成本。\n\n最小化成本为各次环境准备、预热、执行、超时等待、证据检查与清理之和；并行试验共享资源时还要计算争用与隔离费用。若初始有 `n` 个可删除项，每次接受至少删除一项、每版本至多检查 `n` 项，贪心逐项策略最坏为 `O(n²)` 次 oracle 调用，另有初始检查。版本内复用证书可避免无意义重跑，但改变候选身份后不能跨版本直接沿用结论。\n\n有限预算保证及时返回，不保证完整极小性。未知复查若没有新的观察方法，只增加次数并不能保证产生信息。delta debugging 的调用收益取决于分块策略和失败谓词性质；竞态、路径切换与组合故障可能不单调，不能套用无条件的对数次数承诺。\n\n故障注入的资源损失必须按资源种类推导。驻留容量是字节，服务能力是单位时间可完成的资源工作量；即使某资源是唯一限制，两者也不能共用一个比例吞吐公式。\n\n先考虑一个明确的离散驻留模型。`M` 为扣除固定共享占用后可供任务独占的有效内存字节；每任务从启动到结束持续占用 `m>0` 字节，恰运行 `τ>0` 秒；任务始终充足，没有分配、启动、回收开销，且运行时间不随并发变化。可同时运行的任务数为 `q=⌊M/m⌋`，理想饱和完成率为\n\n`X_res(M)=⌊M/m⌋/τ`，单位为任务/秒。\n\n上界来自任意时刻最多容纳 `q` 个任务，每个任务占用一个位置 `τ` 秒；让 `q` 个任务连续成批运行即可在长时间平均上达到它。这里讨论稳态能力，不是任意短窗口的完成数；窗口开始前已经运行的任务会改变短窗口计数。\n\n设每任务独占 3 GB、运行 1 秒，其他资源无限制。10 GB 与整个窗口内保持的 9 GB 都容纳三个任务，所以饱和完成率均为 3 任务/秒；减到 8 GB 才降到 2 任务/秒。由 10 GB 减少 10% 后声称 `X_inject≤3×0.9=2.7`，会被这个合法轨迹反驳。容量影响呈阶梯形；不能仅凭吞吐未下降就排除内存并发限制。若 `M<m`，此模型不能启动任何任务，而不是得到一个小的正吞吐。\n\n另一种情形才支持比例服务上界。设有效服务能力为 `B` 资源工作单位/秒，每个完成任务消耗固定 `w>0` 资源工作单位；没有改变每任务工作量、可用供给计量或任务定义。资源守恒给出 `X≤B/w`。若注入确实使整个稳态窗口的服务能力变为 `B′=(1−c)B`，`0≤c≤1`，则\n\n`X_inject≤(1−c)B/w`。\n\n只有把基线明确规定为同一模型的理想饱和能力 `X_base,sat=B/w`，才能改写为 `X_inject≤(1−c)X_base,sat`。一般实测基线 `X_base,obs` 未必达到该能力，因此不能替换分母。比如原能力为 `10/3` 任务/秒，offered load 只有 2 任务/秒；能力减少 10% 后仍为 3 任务/秒，理想实际完成率仍可为 2，而非必须低于 1.8。理想流体模型下还需计入到达率 `λ`，完成率为 `min(λ,B/w)`。\n\n该服务模型要求核实减少的确是有效工作供给，而不是名义 GPU 份额、字节容量或设备数量。实际注入可能改变缓存、碎片、batch、每任务时长、失败比例和重试工作；这些变化需要重新列账。异构任务还需要按对象大小与生存期做装箱和时间账，不能继续使用同质任务的 `⌊M/m⌋`。恢复流量与其他租户竞争也可能成为新的限制。\n\n日志、输入、核心转储和快照还具有存储、访问与删除成本。高频采集若改变尾延迟，应将机制诊断与最终正常路径性能测量分别报告，并保留采样率、缓冲上限和损失状态。\n\n## 条件变化后的保留、调整与放弃\n\n| 条件变化 | 应保留 | 调整方式与反转信号 |\n|---|---|---|\n| 单进程变为多 stream 异步 | 尝试、stream/event、代次和完成边界 | 扩展 host、设备、消费者三层证据；仅靠最后异常位置时放弃该定位 |\n| 固定输入变为竞态 | 输入、冲突访问、发布关系和调度见证 | 保存交错与试验预算；seed 无法稳定复现时不声称确定性 |\n| 精确数值变为容差或质量契约 | oracle 版本、单位、dtype、首分歧位置 | 增加误差、margin、argmax 和质量判据；不能用宽松容差替代仍要求精确的任务 |\n| 单机变为执行组 | communicator、共同顺序、成员身份与全组终态 | 按恢复协议结束或重建组；局部成功不能报全组成功 |\n| 低频故障变为高频生产故障 | 失败计数、覆盖和样本选择 | 关键边界强制记录，其余有界采样；采集已改变 SLO 时另做正常路径测量 |\n| 测试注入变为生产注入 | 注入边界、影响范围、恢复点与唯一提交权 | 缩小范围或采用影子路径；旧访问未结束时不复用物理对象 |\n| 缓存路径变为直接 I/O | 文件版本、范围和完成语义 | 重核对对齐、部分完成和持久性；读取可见不能替代持久提交 |\n| 字节容量限制变为服务率限制 | 单位、每任务需求与饱和", "excerpt_ranges": [[4000, 7083], [8000, 12000]], "range_unit": "zero-based half-open characters in extracted text", "extracted_characters": 18057, "selected_characters": 7083, "extraction": "UTF-8 text", "selection_reason": "Ranked 4000-character windows by current entry title/tags/questions and algorithm/mechanism keywords; ties retain source order."}, {"path": "knowledge/system-semantics/retrieval-pipeline.md", "sha256": "bd09460502715f214c20ffdd68bdbc6bb9373668a849ca602b75d5e933ff40d0", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-SYS-017：向量检索与 RAG：索引、版本与端到端质量\n\n检索管线把输入转换成查询表示，在约定的候选集合中搜索，再取回材料、重排并交给下游模型。索引的近邻准确率、材料相关性和最终回答质量是三个不同层次；只有把它们连接起来，局部检索加速才有明确意义。\n\n## 查询函数和候选集合先于索引选择\n\n索引侧应固定文档切分、编码器、pooling、normalize、向量维度、数值格式、距离函数以及向量 ID 到材料版本的映射。查询侧应使用配套的编码函数。相同维度只证明张量能相乘，不能证明两个模型产生的坐标空间兼容。\n\n内积取最大值，L2 距离取最小值；是否返回平方 L2 影响阈值解释，即使排序不变。双方单位归一化时，平方 L2=2−2内积，但未归一化向量的范数会影响内积排序。维度缩减、量化和额外变换应同时应用于正确对象，并保留建立精确参考所需的原始向量。\n\n候选集合还受租户、权限、语言、时间和删除状态限制。先搜索全库再过滤固定 top-k，未必得到允许集合中的 top-k；它可能返回不足 k 个，甚至遗漏所有合法近邻。过滤与搜索的结合应是明示的算法选择，性能和 recall 都在相同合法集合上比较。\n\n## 搜索空间裁剪与向量压缩是两条轴\n\nFlat 扫描访问全部向量，便于构造精确参考；批量查询可以把距离计算组织成矩阵运算，但 top-k 选择和输出仍需成本。IVF 在构建时训练粗聚类，查询时选择若干倒排列表；nprobe 控制扫描范围，列表不均会改变实际字节和尾延迟。HNSW 等图索引通过邻接遍历逐步寻找候选，搜索宽度控制探索，但会引入图边、随机访问和构建成本。\n\nPQ 将向量拆成多个子向量，用各自码本编码。查询可以预计算到码本的距离表，在压缩域累加距离，避免把所有向量先恢复成 FP32。码本、查表、残差编码、ID 与列表偏移都要计入容量。PQ 压缩改变距离；IVF/图搜索减少访问范围。两者叠加时，应分别判断误差来自候选没被访问，还是访问后排序被压缩误差改变。\n\n精排可以对一份更大的候选 shortlist 读取原始或较高精度向量重新计算距离。它提高候选内部排序的准确性，却不能找回粗召回从未返回的材料。交叉编码器重排还会改变评分函数，不能把其质量变化解释成近邻搜索误差。\n\nFaiss 固定论文是这些索引机制的来源；它将自身限定为检索库，并不提供一个完整服务所需的并发写入、负载均衡、分片和一致性保证。把索引嵌入 InfraAgents 构建的服务时，这些职责仍需显式设计。\n\n## 构建、发布、更新和查询的生命周期\n\n一个可用索引版本需同时包含编码/变换版本、粗聚类与码本、向量或代码、ID 映射、过滤元数据和材料定位。构建过程中完成某个文件，不代表版本可以被查询；发布点应在这些对象共同完整且质量检查通过之后。查询绑定一个明确版本，读取期间保留其引用，新版本就绪后再切换新查询。\n\n增量添加、删除和更新是否支持，取决于具体索引实现。Faiss 2024-01-16 固定论文说明其当时 HNSW 不支持删除/修改、NSG 不支持增量加入，IVF 可结合 DirectMap 处理 ID 操作；这些是固定实现的边界，不能外推所有同名算法或后续版本。服务可以采用不可变主索引加小增量索引、删除集合和周期重建，但需要处理重复 ID、版本优先级和两路 top-k 合并。\n\n删除标记必须在返回材料前再次得到一致的解释；只从向量索引删掉 ID，而材料缓存仍按旧标识命中，会留下不一致路径。查询中途遇到旧材料失效，应遵循既定快照或明确失败/重查，不能悄悄用新内容替换旧向量对应的材料。\n\n持久索引可由材料和编码器重建，并不意味着重建成本为零。是否保存原始向量、码本训练样本和构建参数，取决于恢复时间目标与可追溯性。内存映射文件也会产生缺页与 I/O，预热后延迟不能直接代表冷服务。\n\n## 分片、缓存与设备放置\n\n按文档 ID 分片时，每个分片搜索后合并候选。若每片都用相同精确距离并返回本地 top-k，合并可得到全局 top-k；近似检索或更小本地候选预算会改变该条件。全局结果需要去重、过滤和确定的同分规则，慢分片缺失不能被当作完整结果。\n\n按 IVF 列表分布可以只访问相关分片，但热点列表可能集中压垮少数机器。复制热点列表增加容量和更新工作；缓存检索结果则依赖查询表示、过滤条件及索引版本。材料缓存与向量缓存应分别计量，命中一个小 ID 列表不代表后续大文本已经在本地。\n\nGPU 更适合较大批量、规则的距离计算与选择，CPU 对小批和不规则遍历有不同成本。选择时要计入编码器输出所在位置、查询搬运、索引驻留、结果回传及和生成模型的 HBM/算力竞争。把检索放上同一 GPU 并不自动减少完整 RAG 延迟。\n\n## RAG 的时间与质量链\n\n完整在线链为入口排队、查询编码、候选搜索、材料读取、重排、截断与排列、prompt 构造、生成 prefill、首 token 提交。各阶段可以有并行或流水，但只有实际依赖允许时才取最大值，串行阶段必须相加。只测 prefill 的 TTFT 应明确起点；用户端 TTFT 包含前面的检索链。\n\n增加候选数会影响搜索、材料读取、重排和最终上下文长度。减少检索数可能降低时延，同时丢失回答所需证据；扩大上下文也不保证质量单调上升。固定材料集合与顺序的系统比较，用来评价执行优化；改变候选、重排、压缩或片段复用策略的比较，则需要端到端质量约束。\n\n非前缀 KV 复用可接在材料组装之后，但文档向量的独立可缓存性不能传递成 decoder KV 的独立可缓存性。材料位置、顺序与前文改变后，要按上下文复用章判断精确前缀或近似融合，而不是直接拼接状态。\n\n## C12-B：检索与 RAG 的完整验证场景\n\n设定 N=1,000,000、d=768、FP32，Flat 向量本体为 3,072,000,000 字节，约 2.86 GiB。候选 A 是 Flat 精确扫描；候选 B 是 4,096 列表的 IVF，加 96 个 8-bit 子量化器。B 的代码为 96,000,000 字节、64-bit ID 为 8,000,000 字节、粗中心为 12,582,912 字节，PQ 码本为 786,432 字节，合计 117,369,344 字节，尚未含列表结构、变换、过滤、工作区与高精度精排副本。若另外保存全部原向量，不能仍宣称整个系统只用这个压缩容量。\n\n在列表完全均匀的设定中，nprobe=16 平均扫描 1,000,000×16/4,096=3,906.25 个向量，还要计算粗中心距离和 query 查表；真实列表偏斜、过滤与并发需重新测量。把 nprobe 提高到 32、64，画出同一查询集的 recall—p99—内存关系，不预设某个值满足质量。\n\n独立距离算例取单位查询 q=(1,0)，数据库向量 a=(1,0)、b=(0.8,0.6)、c=(0,1)。内积依次为 1、0.8、0，平方 L2 为 0、0.4、2。禁用 a 后合法 top-1 应为 b；“全库 top-1 再过滤”的空结果不是正确参考。构造重复 ID、相同距离、更新文档、删除、空库、k 超过合法候选数及缺失分片，检查结果语义。\n\n质量分三层：ANN recall 对固定原向量的精确合法近邻；材料检索/重排用有相关性标注的 recall、MRR/nDCG；最终回答用任务正确性与证据一致性。前两层通过不保证答案通过。索引训练集、调参查询与最终测试查询分开，加入新领域和冷材料分布以检查偏移。\n\n一个时序设定为查询编码 5 ms、搜索 8 ms、取材料 12 ms、重排 30 ms、构造 2 ms、模型 prefill 并生成首 token 80 ms、提交 5 ms；完全串行且不含排队时为 142 ms。若优化把搜索 8 ms 降到 4 ms，其他不变，整体只下降 4 ms。所有数字是验证预算的设定，不是目标机器性能。高复用稳定库可以承担较重索引构建；频繁更新、小库或严格 recall 则可能让 Flat/简单增量方案更合适。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- [S-FAISS：The Faiss "c525baf78f19e7dca3ca52b06d93401b87cc17b2e90c5dae132190216611c2ee", "reading_scope": "excerpt", "category": "source_original", "content": "[EXCERPT extracted-character range 0:62072]\n=== PDF page 1 ===\nCeph: A Scalable, High-Performance Distributed File System\nSage A. Weil\nScott A. Brandt\nEthan L. Miller\nDarrell D. E. Long\nCarlos Maltzahn\nUniversity of California, Santa Cruz\n{sage, scott, elm, darrell, carlosm}@cs.ucsc.edu\nAbstract\nWe have developed Ceph, a distributed ﬁle system that\nprovides excellent performance, reliability, and scala-\nbility.\nCeph maximizes the separation between data\nand metadata management by replacing allocation ta-\nbles with a pseudo-random data distribution function\n(CRUSH) designed for heterogeneous and dynamic clus-\nters of unreliable object storage devices (OSDs).\nWe\nleverage device intelligence by distributing data replica-\ntion, failure detection and recovery to semi-autonomous\nOSDs running a specialized local object ﬁle system. A\ndynamic distributed metadata cluster provides extremely\nefﬁcient metadata management and seamlessly adapts to\na wide range of general purpose and scientiﬁc comput-\ning ﬁle system workloads. Performance measurements\nunder a variety of workloads show that Ceph has ex-\ncellent I/O performance and scalable metadata manage-\nment, supporting more than 250,000 metadata operations\nper second.\n1\nIntroduction\nSystem designers have long sought to improve the per-\nformance of ﬁle systems, which have proved critical to\nthe overall performance of an exceedingly broad class of\napplications. The scientiﬁc and high-performance com-\nputing communities in particular have driven advances\nin the performance and scalability of distributed stor-\nage systems, typically predicting more general purpose\nneeds by a few years. Traditional solutions, exempliﬁed\nby NFS [20], provide a straightforward model in which\na server exports a ﬁle system hierarchy that clients can\nmap into their local name space. Although widely used,\nthe centralization inherent in the client/server model has\nproven a signiﬁcant obstacle to scalable performance.\nMore recent distributed ﬁle systems have adopted ar-\nchitectures based on object-based storage, in which con-\nventional hard disks are replaced with intelligent object\nstorage devices (OSDs) which combine a CPU, network\ninterface, and local cache with an underlying disk or\nRAID [4, 7, 8, 32, 35]. OSDs replace the traditional\nblock-level interface with one in which clients can read\nor write byte ranges to much larger (and often variably\nsized) named objects, distributing low-level block allo-\ncation decisions to the devices themselves. Clients typ-\nically interact with a metadata server (MDS) to perform\nmetadata operations (open, rename), while communicat-\ning directly with OSDs to perform ﬁle I/O (reads and\nwrites), signiﬁcantly improving overall scalability.\nSystems adopting this model continue to suffer from\nscalability limitations due to little or no distribution of\nthe metadata workload. Continued reliance on traditional\nﬁle system principles like allocation lists and inode ta-\nbles and a reluctance to delegate intelligence to the OSDs\nhave further limited scalability and performance, and in-\ncreased the cost of reliability.\nWe present Ceph, a distributed ﬁle system that pro-\nvides excellent performanceand reliability while promis-\ning unparalleled scalability. Our architecture is based on\nthe assumption that systems at the petabyte scale are in-\nherently dynamic: large systems are inevitably built in-\ncrementally, node failures are the norm rather than the\nexception, and the quality and character of workloads are\nconstantly shifting over time.\nCeph decouples data and metadata operations by elim-\ninating ﬁle allocation tables and replacing them with gen-\nerating functions. This allows Ceph to leverage the in-\ntelligence present in OSDs to distribute the complexity\nsurrounding data access, update serialization, replication\nand reliability, failure detection, and recovery. Ceph uti-\nlizes a highly adaptive distributed metadata cluster ar-\nchitecture that dramatically improves the scalability of\nmetadata access, and with it, the scalability of the en-\ntire system. We discuss the goals and workload assump-\ntions motivating our choices in the design of the architec-\nture, analyze their impact on system scalability and per-\nformance, and relate our experiences in implementing a\nfunctional system prototype.\nOSDI ’06: 7th USENIX Symposium on Operating Systems Design and Implementation\nUSENIX Association\n307\n\n\n=== PDF page 2 ===\nMetadata\nstorage\nFile I/O\nMetadata Cluster\nObject Storage Cluster\nclient\nbash\nLinux kernel\nfuse\nvfs\nlibfuse\nls\n…\nclient\nbash\nLinux kernel\nfuse\nvfs\nlibfuse\nls\n…\nmyproc\nclient\nmyproc\nclient\nClients\nMetadata operations\nFigure 1: System architecture. Clients perform ﬁle I/O\nby communicating directly with OSDs. Each process can\neither link directly to a client instance or interact with a\nmounted ﬁle system.\n2\nSystem Overview\nThe Ceph ﬁle system has three main components: the\nclient, each instance of which exposes a near-POSIX ﬁle\nsystem interface to a host or process; a cluster of OSDs,\nwhich collectively stores all data and metadata; and a\nmetadata server cluster, which manages the namespace\n(ﬁle names and directories) while coordinating security,\nconsistency and coherence (see Figure 1). We say the\nCeph interface is near-POSIX because we ﬁnd it appro-\npriate to extend the interface and selectively relax con-\nsistency semantics in order to better align with the needs\nof applications and to improve system performance.\nThe primary goals of the architecture are scalability (to\nhundreds of petabytes and beyond), performance, and re-\nliability. Scalability is considered in a variety of dimen-\nsions, including the overall storage capacity and through-\nput of the system, and performance in terms of individ-\nual clients, directories, or ﬁles. Our target workload may\ninclude such extreme cases as tens or hundreds of thou-\nsands of hosts concurrently reading from or writing to\nthe same ﬁle or creating ﬁles in the same directory. Such\nscenarios, common in scientiﬁc applications running on\nsupercomputing clusters, are increasingly indicative of\ntomorrow’s general purpose workloads.\nMore impor-\ntantly, we recognize that distributed ﬁle system work-\nloads are inherently dynamic, with signiﬁcant variation\nin data and metadata access as active applications and\ndata sets change over time. Ceph directly addresses the\nissue of scalability while simultaneously achieving high\nperformance, reliability and availability through three\nfundamental design features: decoupled data and meta-\ndata, dynamic distributed metadata management, and re-\nliable autonomic distributed object storage.\nDecoupled Data and Metadata—Ceph maximizes the\nseparation of ﬁle metadata management from the storage\nof ﬁle data. Metadata operations (open, rename, etc.)\nare collectively managed by a metadata server cluster,\nwhile clients interact directly with OSDs to perform ﬁle\nI/O (reads and writes). Object-based storage has long\npromised to improve the scalability of ﬁle systems by\ndelegating low-level block allocation decisions to indi-\nvidual devices. However, in contrast to existing object-\nbased ﬁle systems [4, 7, 8, 32] which replace long per-ﬁle\nblock lists with shorter object lists, Ceph eliminates allo-\ncation lists entirely. Instead, ﬁle data is striped onto pre-\ndictably named objects, while a special-purpose data dis-\ntribution function called CRUSH [29] assigns objects to\nstorage devices. This allows any party to calculate (rather\nthan look up) the name and location of objects compris-\ning a ﬁle’s contents, eliminating the need to maintain and\ndistribute object lists, simplifying the design of the sys-\ntem, and reducing the metadata cluster workload.\nDynamic\nDistributed\nMetadata\nManagement—\nBecause ﬁle system metadata operations make up as\nmuch as half of typical ﬁle system workloads [22],\neffective metadata management is critical to overall\nsystem performance.\nCeph utilizes a novel metadata\ncluster architecture based on Dynamic Subtree Parti-\ntioning [30] that adaptively and intelligently distributes\nresponsibility for managing the ﬁle system directory\nhierarchy among tens or even hundreds of MDSs.\nA\n(dynamic) hierarchical partition preserves locality in\neach MDS’s workload, facilitating efﬁcient updates\nand aggressive prefetching to improve performance\nfor common workloads.\nSigniﬁcantly, the workload\ndistribution among metadata servers is based entirely\non current access patterns, allowing Ceph to effectively\nutilize available MDS resources under any workload and\nachieve near-linear scaling in the number of MDSs.\nReliable Autonomic Distributed Object Storage—\nLarge systems composed of many thousands of devices\nare inherently dynamic: they are built incrementally, they\ngrow and contract as new storage is deployed and old de-\nvices are decommissioned, device failures are frequent\nand expected, and large volumes of data are created,\nmoved, and deleted. All of these factors require that the\ndistribution of data evolve to effectively utilize available\nresources and maintain the desired level of data replica-\ntion. Ceph delegates responsibility for data migration,\nreplication, failure detection, and failure recovery to the\ncluster of OSDs that store the data, while at a high level,\nOSDs collectively provide a single logical object store\nto clients and metadata servers. This approach allows\nCeph to more effectively leverage the intelligence (CPU\nand memory) present on each OSD to achieve reliable,\nhighly available object storage with linear scaling.\nWe describe the operation of the Ceph client, metadata\nserver cluster, and distributed object store, and how they\nare affected by the critical features of our architecture.\nWe also describe the status of our prototype.\n3\nClient Operation\nWe introduce the overall operation of Ceph’s compo-\nnents and their interaction with applications by describ-\nOSDI ’06: 7th USENIX Symposium on Operating Systems Design and Implementation\nUSENIX Association\n308\n\n\n=== PDF page 3 ===\ning Ceph’s client operation. The Ceph client runs on\neach host executing application code and exposes a ﬁle\nsystem interface to applications. In the Ceph prototype,\nthe client code runs entirely in user space and can be ac-\ncessed either by linking to it directly or as a mounted\nﬁle system via FUSE [25] (a user-space ﬁle system in-\nterface). Each client maintains its own ﬁle data cache,\nindependent of the kernel page or buffer caches, making\nit accessible to applications that link to the client directly.\n3.1\nFile I/O and Capabilities\nWhen a process opens a ﬁle, the client sends a request\nto the MDS cluster. An MDS traverses the ﬁle system\nhierarchy to translate the ﬁle name into the ﬁle inode,\nwhich includes a unique inode number, the ﬁle owner,\nmode, size, and other per-ﬁle metadata. If the ﬁle exists\nand access is granted, the MDS returns the inode num-\nber, ﬁle size, and information about the striping strategy\nused to map ﬁle data into objects. The MDS may also\nissue the client a capability (if it does not already have\none) specifying which operations are permitted. Capa-\nbilities currently include four bits controlling the client’s\nability to read, cache reads, write, and buffer writes. In\nthe future, capabilities will include security keys allow-\ning clients to prove to OSDs that they are authorized to\nread or write data [13, 19] (the prototype currently trusts\nall clients). Subsequent MDS involvement in ﬁle I/O is\nlimited to managing capabilities to preserve ﬁle consis-\ntency and achieve proper semantics.\nCeph generalizes a range of striping strategies to map\nﬁle data onto a sequence of objects. To avoid any need\nfor ﬁle allocation metadata, object names simply com-\nbine the ﬁle inode number and the stripe number. Ob-\nject replicas are then assigned to OSDs using CRUSH,\na globally known mapping function (described in Sec-\ntion 5.1). For example, if one or more clients open a ﬁle\nfor read access, an MDS grants them the capability to\nread and cache ﬁle content. Armed with the inode num-\nber, layout, and ﬁle size, the clients can name and locate\nall objects containing ﬁle data and read directly from the\nOSD cluster. Any objects or byte ranges that don’t ex-\nist are deﬁned to be ﬁle “holes,” or zeros. Similarly, if a\nclient opens a ﬁle for writing, it is granted the capability\nto write with buffering, and any data it generates at any\noffset in the ﬁle is simply written to the appropriate ob-\nject on the appropriate OSD. The client relinquishes the\ncapability on ﬁle close and provides the MDS with the\nnew ﬁle size (the largest offset written), which redeﬁnes\nthe set of objects that (may) exist and contain ﬁle data.\n3.2\nClient Synchronization\nPOSIX semantics sensibly require that reads reﬂect any\ndata previously written, and that writes are atomic (i. e.,\nthe result of overlapping, concurrent writes will reﬂect a\nparticular order of occurrence). When a ﬁle is opened by\nmultiple clients with either multiple writers or a mix of\nreaders and writers, the MDS will revoke any previously\nissued read caching and write buffering capabilities,\nforcing client I/O for that ﬁle to be synchronous. That\nis, each application read or write operation will block\nuntil it is acknowledged by the OSD, effectively plac-\ning the burden of update serialization and synchroniza-\ntion with the OSD storing each object. When writes span\nobject boundaries, clients acquire exclusive locks on the\naffected objects (granted by their respective OSDs), and\nimmediately submit the write and unlock operations to\nachieve the desired serialization. Object locks are simi-\nlarly used to mask latency for large writes by acquiring\nlocks and ﬂushing data asynchronously.\nNot surprisingly, synchronous I/O can be a perfor-\nmance killer for applications, particularly those doing\nsmall reads or writes, due to the latency penalty—at least\none round-trip to the OSD. Although read-write sharing\nis relatively rare in general-purpose workloads [22], it is\nmore common in scientiﬁc computing applications [27],\nwhere performance is often critical. For this reason, it\nis often desirable to relax consistency at the expense of\nstrict standards conformance in situations where appli-\ncations do not rely on it. Although Ceph supports such\nrelaxation via a global switch, and many other distributed\nﬁle systems punt on this issue [20], this is an imprecise\nand unsatisfying solution: either performance suffers, or\nconsistency is lost system-wide.\nFor precisely this reason, a set of high perfor-\nmance computing extensions to the POSIX I/O interface\nhave been proposed by the high-performance computing\n(HPC) community [31], a subset of which are imple-\nmented by Ceph. Most notably, these include an O LAZY\nﬂag for open that allows applications to explicitly relax\nthe usual coherency requirements for a shared-write ﬁle.\nPerformance-conscious applications which manage their\nown consistency (e. g., by writing to different parts of\nthe same ﬁle, a common pattern in HPC workloads [27])\nare then allowed to buffer writes or cache reads when\nI/O would otherwise be performed synchronously. If de-\nsired, applications can then explicitly synchronize with\ntwo additional calls: lazyio propagate will ﬂush a given\nbyte range to the object store, while lazyio synchronize\nwill ensure that the effects of previous propagations are\nreﬂected in any subsequent reads. The Ceph synchro-\nnization model thus retains its simplicity by providing\ncorrect read-write and shared-write semantics between\nclients via synchronous I/O, and extending the applica-\ntion interface to relax consistency for performance con-\nscious distributed applications.\n3.3\nNamespace Operations\nClient interaction with the ﬁle system namespace is man-\naged by the metadata server cluster. Both read operations\nOSDI ’06: 7th USENIX Symposium on Operating Systems Design and Implementation\nUSENIX Association\n309\n\n\n=== PDF page 4 ===\n(e. g., readdir, stat) and updates (e. g., unlink, chmod) are\nsynchronously applied by the MDS to ensure serializa-\ntion, consistency, correct security, and safety. For sim-\nplicity, no metadata locks or leases are issued to clients.\nFor HPC workloads in particular, callbacks offer mini-\nmal upside at a high potential cost in complexity.\nInstead, Ceph optimizes for the most common meta-\ndata access scenarios. A readdir followed by a stat of\neach ﬁle (e. g., ls -l) is an extremely common access\npattern and notorious performance killer in large direc-\ntories. A readdir in Ceph requires only a single MDS\nrequest, which fetches the entire directory, including in-\node contents. By default, if a readdir is immediately\nfollowed by one or more stats, the brieﬂy cached infor-\nmation is returned; otherwise it is discarded. Although\nthis relaxes coherence slightly in that an intervening in-\node modiﬁcation may go unnoticed, we gladly make this\ntrade for vastly improved performance. This behavior\nis explicitly captured by the readdirplus [31] extension,\nwhich returns lstat results with directory entries (as some\nOS-speciﬁc implementations of getdir already do).\nCeph could allow consistency to be further relaxed by\ncaching metadata longer, much like earlier versions of\nNFS, which typically cache for 30 seconds. However,\nthis approach breaks coherency in a way that is often crit-\nical to applications, such as those using stat to determine\nif a ﬁle has been updated—they either behave incorrectly,\nor end up waiting for old cached values to time out.\nWe opt instead to again provide correct behavior and\nextend the interface in instances where it adversely af-\nfects performance. This choice is most clearly illustrated\nby a stat operation on a ﬁle currently opened by multiple\nclients for writing. In order to return a correct ﬁle size\nand modiﬁcation time, the MDS revokes any write ca-\npabilities to momentarily stop updates and collect up-to-\ndate size and mtime values from all writers. The highest\nvalues are returned with the stat reply, and capabilities\nare reissued to allow further progress. Although stop-\nping multiple writers may seem drastic, it is necessary to\nensure proper serializability. (For a single writer, a cor-\nrect value can be retrieved from the writing client without\ninterrupting progress.) Applications for which coherent\nbehavior is unnecesssary—victims of a POSIX interface\nthat doesn’t align with their needs—can use statlite [31],\nwhich takes a bit mask specifying which inode ﬁelds are\nnot required to be coherent.\n4\nDynamically Distributed Metadata\nMetadata operations often make up as much as half of ﬁle\nsystem workloads [22] and lie in the critical path, making\nthe MDS cluster critical to overall performance. Meta-\ndata management also presents a critical scaling chal-\nlenge in distributed ﬁle systems: although capacity and\naggregate I/O rates can scale almost arbitrarily with the\naddition of more storage devices, metadata operations\ninvolve a greater degree of interdependence that makes\nscalable consistency and coherence management more\ndifﬁcult.\nFile and directory metadata in Ceph is very small, con-\nsisting almost entirely of directory entries (ﬁle names)\nand inodes (80 bytes). Unlike conventional ﬁle systems,\nno ﬁle allocation metadata is necessary—object names\nare constructed using the inode number, and distributed\nto OSDs using CRUSH. This simpliﬁes the metadata\nworkload and allows our MDS to efﬁciently manage a\nvery large working set of ﬁles, independent of ﬁle sizes.\nOur design further seeks to minimize metadata related\ndisk I/O through the use of a two-tiered storage strategy,\nand to maximize locality and cache efﬁciency with Dy-\nnamic Subtree Partitioning [30].\n4.1\nMetadata Storage\nAlthough the MDS cluster aims to satisfy most requests\nfrom its in-memory cache, metadata updates must be\ncommitted to disk for safety. A set of large, bounded,\nlazily ﬂushed journals allows each MDS to quickly\nstream its updated metadata to the OSD cluster in an ef-\nﬁcient and distributed manner. The per-MDS journals,\neach many hundreds of megabytes, also absorb repeti-\ntive metadata updates (common to most workloads) such\nthat when old journal entries are eventually ﬂushed to\nlong-term storage, many are already rendered obsolete.\nAlthough MDS recovery is not yet implemented by our\nprototype, the journals are designed such that in the event\nof an MDS failure, another node can quickly rescan the\njournal to recover the critical contents of the failed node’s\nin-memory cache (for quick startup) and in doing so re-\ncover the ﬁle system state.\nThis strategy provides the best of both worlds: stream-\ning updates to disk in an efﬁcient (sequential) fashion,\nand a vastly reduced re-write workload, allowing the\nlong-term on-disk storage layout to be optimized for fu-\nture read access. In particular, inodes are embedded di-\nrectly within directories, allowing the MDS to prefetch\nentire directories with a single OSD read request and\nexploit the high degree of directory locality present in\nmost workloads [22]. Each directory’s content is writ-\nten to the OSD cluster using the same striping and dis-\ntribution strategy as metadata journals and ﬁle data. In-\node numbers are allocated in ranges to metadata servers\nand considered immutable in our prototype, although in\nthe future they could be trivially reclaimed on ﬁle dele-\ntion. An auxiliary anchor table [28] keeps the rare inode\nwith multiple hard links globally addressable by inode\nnumber—all without encumbering the overwhelmingly\ncommon case of singly-linked ﬁles with an enormous,\nsparsely populated and cumbersome inode table.\nOSDI ’06: 7th USENIX Symposium on Operating Systems Design and Implementation\nUSENIX Association\n310\n\n\n=== PDF page 5 ===\nRoot\nMDS 0\nMDS 4\nMDS 1\nMDS 2\nMDS 3\nBusy directory hashed across many MDS’s\nFigure 2: Ceph dynamically maps subtrees of the direc-\ntory hierarchy to metadata servers based on the current\nworkload. Individual directories are hashed across mul-\ntiple nodes only when they become hot spots.\n4.2\nDynamic Subtree Partitioning\nOur primary-copy caching strategy makes a single au-\nthoritative MDS responsible for managing cache coher-\nence and serializing updates for any given piece of meta-\ndata. While most existing distributed ﬁle systems employ\nsome form of static subtree-based partitioning to delegate\nthis authority (usually forcing an administrator to carve\nthe dataset into smaller static “volumes”), some recent\nand experimental ﬁle systems have used hash functions\nto distribute directory and ﬁle metadata [4], effectively\nsacriﬁcing locality for load distribution. Both approaches\nhave critical limitations: static subtree partitioning fails\nto cope with dynamic workloads and data sets, while\nhashing destroys metadata locality and critical opportu-\nnities for efﬁcient metadata prefetching and storage.\nCeph’s MDS cluster is based on a dynamic sub-\ntree partitioning strategy [30] that adaptively distributes\ncached metadata hierarchically across a set of nodes, as\nillustrated in Figure 2. Each MDS measures the popu-\nlarity of metadata within the directory hierarchy using\ncounters with an exponential time decay. Any opera-\ntion increments the counter on the affected inode and all\nof its ancestors up to the root directory, providing each\nMDS with a weighted tree describing the recent load dis-\ntribution. MDS load values are periodically compared,\nand appropriately-sized subtrees of the directory hierar-\nchy are migrated to keep the workload evenly distributed.\nThe combination of shared long-term storage and care-\nfully constructed namespace locks allows such migra-\ntions to proceed by transferring the appropriate contents\nof the in-memory cache to the new authority, with mini-\nmal impact on coherence locks or client capabilities. Im-\nported metadata is written to the new MDS’s journal for\nsafety, while additional journal entries on both ends en-\nsure that the transfer of authority is invulnerable to in-\ntervening failures (similar to a two-phase commit). The\nresulting subtree-based partition is kept coarse to mini-\nmize preﬁx replication overhead and to preserve locality.\nWhen metadata is replicated across multiple MDS\nnodes, inode contents are separated into three groups,\neach with different consistency semantics:\nsecurity\n(owner, mode), ﬁle (size, mtime), and immutable (inode\nnumber, ctime, layout). While immutable ﬁelds never\nchange, security and ﬁle locks are governed by inde-\npendent ﬁnite state machines, each with a different set\nof states and transitions designed to accommodate dif-\nferent access and update patterns while minimizing lock\ncontention. For example, owner and mode are required\nfor the security check during path traversal but rarely\nchange, requiring very few states, while the ﬁle lock re-\nﬂects a wider range of client access modes as it controls\nan MDS’s ability to issue client capabilities.\n4.3\nTrafﬁc Control\nPartitioning the directory hierarchy across multiple\nnodes can balance a broad range of workloads, but can-\nnot always cope with hot spots or ﬂash crowds, where\nmany clients access the same directory or ﬁle. Ceph uses\nits knowledge of metadata popularity to provide a wide\ndistribution for hot spots only when needed and with-\nout incurring the associated overhead and loss of direc-\ntory locality in the general case. The contents of heavily\nread directories (e. g., many opens) are selectively repli-\ncated across multiple nodes to distribute load. Directo-\nries that are particularly large or experiencing a heavy\nwrite workload (e.g., many ﬁle creations) have their con-\ntents hashed by ﬁle name across the cluster, achieving a\nbalanced distribution at the expense of directory local-\nity. This adaptive approach allows Ceph to encompass\na broad spectrum of partition granularities, capturing the\nbeneﬁts of both coarse and ﬁne partitions in the speciﬁc\ncircumstances and portions of the ﬁle system where those\nstrategies are most effective.\nEvery MDS response provides the client with updated\ninformation about the authority and any replication of the\nrelevant inode and its ancestors, allowing clients to learn\nthe metadata partition for the parts of the ﬁle system with\nwhich they interact. Future metadata operations are di-\nrected at the authority (for updates) or a random replica\n(for reads) based on the deepest known preﬁx of a given\npath. Normally clients learn the locations of unpopular\n(unreplicated) metadata and are able to contact the appro-\npriate MDS directly. Clients accessing popular metadata,\nhowever, are told the metadata reside either on different\nor multiple MDS nodes, effectively bounding the num-\nber of clients believing any particular piece of metadata\nresides on any particular MDS, dispersing potential hot\nspots and ﬂash crowds before they occur.\n5\nDistributed Object Storage\nFrom a high level, Ceph clients and metadata servers\nview the object storage cluster (possibly tens or hundreds\nof thousands of OSDs) as a single logical object store\nand namespace. Ceph’s Reliable Autonomic Distributed\nObject Store (RADOS) achieves linear scaling in both\nOSDI ’06: 7th USENIX Symposium on Operating Systems Design and Implementation\nUSENIX Association\n311\n\n\n=== PDF page 6 ===\n…\n…\n…\n…\n…\n…\nCRUSH(pgid)\n(osd1, osd2)\nOSDs\n(grouped by\nfailure domain)\nFile\nObjects\nhash(oid) & mask\npgid\nPGs\n(ino,ono)\noid\nFigure 3: Files are striped across many objects, grouped\ninto placement groups (PGs), and distributed to OSDs\nvia CRUSH, a specialized replica placement function.\ncapacity and aggregate performance by delegating man-\nagement of object replication, cluster expansion, failure\ndetection and recovery to OSDs in a distributed fashion.\n5.1\nData Distribution with CRUSH\nCeph must distribute petabytes of data among an evolv-\ning cluster of thousands of storage devices such that de-\nvice storage and bandwidth resources are effectively uti-\nlized. In order to avoid imbalance (e. g., recently de-\nployed devices mostly idle or empty) or load asymme-\ntries (e. g., new, hot data on new devices only), we adopt\na strategy that distributes new data randomly, migrates a\nrandom subsample of existing data to new devices, and\nuniformly redistributes data from removed devices. This\nstochastic approach is robust in that it performs equally\nwell under any potential workload.\nCeph ﬁrst maps objects into placement groups (PGs)\nusing a simple hash function, with an adjustable bit mask\nto control the number of PGs. We choose a value that\ngives each OSD on the order of 100 PGs to balance vari-\nance in OSD utilizations with the amount of replication-\nrelated metadata maintained by each OSD. Placement\ngroups are then assigned to OSDs using CRUSH (Con-\ntrolled Replication Under Scalable Hashing) [29], a\npseudo-random data distribution function that efﬁciently\nmaps each PG to an ordered list of OSDs upon which to\nstore object replicas. This differs from conventional ap-\nproaches (including other object-based ﬁle systems) in\nthat data placement does not rely on any block or ob-\nject list metadata. To locate any object, CRUSH requires\nonly the placement group and an OSD cluster map: a\ncompact, hierarchical description of the devices compris-\ning the storage cluster. This approach has two key ad-\nvantages: ﬁrst, it is completely distributed such that any\nparty (client, OSD, or MDS) can independently calcu-\nlate the location of any object; and second, the map is\ninfrequently updated, virtually eliminating any exchange\nof distribution-related metadata. In doing so, CRUSH si-\nmultaneously solves both the data distribution problem\n(“where should I store data”) and the data location prob-\nlem (“where did I store data”). By design, small changes\nto the storage cluster have little impact on existing PG\nmappings, minimizing data migration due to device fail-\nures or cluster expansion.\nThe cluster map hierarchy is structured to align with\nthe clusters physical or logical composition and potential\nsources of failure. For instance, one might form a four-\nlevel hierarchy for an installation consisting of shelves\nfull of OSDs, rack cabinets full of shelves, and rows of\ncabinets. Each OSD also has a weight value to control\nthe relative amount of data it is assigned. CRUSH maps\nPGs onto OSDs based on placement rules, which de-\nﬁne the level of replication and any constraints on place-\nment. For example, one might replicate each PG on three\nOSDs, all situated in the same row (to limit inter-row\nreplication trafﬁc) but separated into different cabinets\n(to minimize exposure to a power circuit or edge switch\nfailure). The cluster map also includes a list of down\nor inactive devices and an epoch number, which is incre-\nmented each time the map changes. All OSD requests are\ntagged with the client’s map epoch, such that all parties\ncan agree on the current distribution of data. Incremental\nmap updates are shared between cooperating OSDs, and\npiggyback on OSD replies if the client’s map is out of\ndate.\n5.2\nReplication\nIn contrast to systems like Lustre [4], which assume one\ncan construct sufﬁciently reliable OSDs using mecha-\nnisms like RAID or fail-over on a SAN, we assume that\nin a petabyte or exabyte system failure will be the norm\nrather than the exception, and at any point in time several\nOSDs are likely to be inoperable. To maintain system\navailability and ensure data safety in a scalable fashion,\nRADOS manages its own replication of data using a vari-\nant of primary-copy replication [2], while taking steps to\nminimize the impact on performance.\nData is replicated in terms of placement groups, each\nof which is mapped to an ordered list of n OSDs (for\nn-way replication). Clients send all writes to the ﬁrst\nnon-failed OSD in an object’s PG (the primary), which\nassigns a new version number for the object and PG and\nforwards the write to any additional replica OSDs. After\neach replica has applied the update and responded to the\nprimary, the primary applies the update locally and the\nwrite is acknowledged to the client. Reads are directed\nat the primary. This approach spares the client of any of\nthe complexity surrounding synchronization or serializa-\ntion between replicas, which can be onerous in the pres-\nence of other writers or failure recovery. It also shifts the\nbandwidth consumed by replication from the client to the\nOSD cluster’s internal network, where we expect greater\nresources to be available. Intervening replica OSD fail-\nures are ignored, as any subsequent recovery (see Sec-\ntion 5.5) will reliably restore replica consistency.\nOSDI ’06: 7th USENIX Symposium on Operating Systems Design and Implementation\nUSENIX Association\n312\n\n\n=== PDF page 7 ===\nWrite\nApply update\nAck\nCommit to disk\nCommit\nTime\nClient\nPrimary\nReplica\nReplica\nFigure 4: RADOS responds with an ack after the write\nhas been applied to the buffer caches on all OSDs repli-\ncating the object. Only after it has been safely committed\nto disk is a ﬁnal commit notiﬁcation sent to the client.\n5.3\nData Safety\nIn distributed storage systems, there are essentially two\nreasons why data is written to shared storage.\nFirst,\nclients are interested in making their updates visible to\nother clients. This should be quick: writes should be vis-\nible as soon as possible, particularly when multiple writ-\ners or mixed readers and writers force clients to operate\nsynchronously. Second, clients are interested in know-\ning deﬁnitively that the data they’ve written is safely\nreplicated, on disk, and will survive power or other fail-\nures. RADOS disassociates synchronization from safety\nwhen acknowledging updates, allowing Ceph to realize\nboth low-latency updates for efﬁcient application syn-\nchronization and well-deﬁned data safety semantics.\nFigure 4 illustrates the messages sent during an ob-\nject write. The primary forwards the update to replicas,\nand replies with an ack after it is applied to all OSDs’\nin-memory buffer caches, allowing synchronous POSIX\ncalls on the client to return. A ﬁnal commit is sent (per-\nhaps many seconds later) when data is safely committed\nto disk. We send the ack to the client only after the up-\ndate is fully replicated to seamlessly tolerate the failure\nof any single OSD, even though this increases client la-\ntency. By default, clients also buffer writes until they\ncommit to avoid data loss in the event of a simultaneous\npower loss to all OSDs in the placement group. When\nrecovering in such cases, RADOS allows the replay of\npreviously acknowledged (and thus ordered) updates for\na ﬁxed interval before new updates are accepted.\n5.4\nFailure Detection\nTimely failure detection is critical to maintaining data\nsafety, but can become difﬁcult as a cluster scales to\nmany thousands of devices. For certain failures, such\nas disk errors or corrupted data, OSDs can self-report.\nFailures that make an OSD unreachable on the network,\nhowever, require active monitoring, which RADOS dis-\ntributes by having each OSD monitor those peers with\nwhich it shares PGs. In most cases, existing replication\ntrafﬁc serves as a passive conﬁrmation of liveness, with\nno additional communication overhead. If an OSD has\nnot heard from a peer recently, an explicit ping is sent.\nRADOS considers two dimensions of OSD liveness:\nwhether the OSD is reachable, and whether it is as-\nsigned data by CRUSH. An unresponsive OSD is initially\nmarked down, and any primary responsibilities (update\nserialization, replication) temporarily pass to the next\nOSD in each of its placement groups. If the OSD does\nnot quickly recover, it is marked out of the data distribu-\ntion, and another OSD joins each PG to re-replicate its\ncontents. Clients which have pending operations with a\nfailed OSD simply resubmit to the new primary.\nBecause a wide variety of network anomalies may\ncause intermittent lapses in OSD connectivity, a small\ncluster of monitors collects failure reports and ﬁlters\nout transient or systemic problems (like a network parti-\ntion) centrally. Monitors (which are only partially imple-\nmented) use elections, active peer monitoring, short-term\nleases, and two-phase commits to collectively provide\nconsistent and available access to the cluster map. When\nthe map is updated to reﬂect any failures or recoveries,\naffected OSDs are provided incremental map updates,\nwhich then spread throughout the cluster by piggyback-\ning on existing inter-OSD communication. Distributed\ndetection allows fast detection without unduly burden-\ning monitors, while resolving the occurrence of incon-\nsistency with centralized arbitration. Most importantly,\nRADOS avoids initiating widespread data re-replication\ndue to systemic problems by marking OSDs down but\nnot out (e. g., after a power loss to half of all OSDs).\n5.5\nRecovery and Cluster Updates\nThe OSD cluster map will change due to OSD failures,\nrecoveries, and explicit cluster changes such as the de-\nployment of new storage. Ceph handles all such changes\nin the same way. To facilitate fast recovery, OSDs main-\ntain a version number for each object and a log of re-\ncent changes (names and versions of updated or deleted\nobjects) for each PG (similar to the replication logs in\nHarp [14]) .\nWhen an active OSD receives an updated cluster map,\nit iterates over all locally stored placement groups and\ncalculates the CRUSH mapping to determine which ones\nit is responsible for, either as a primary or replica. If a\nPG’s membership has changed, or if the OSD has just\nbooted, the OSD must peer with the PG’s other OSDs.\nFor replicated PGs, the OSD provides the primary with\nits current PG version number. If the OSD is the primary\nfor the PG, it collects current (and former) replicas’ PG\nversions. If the primary lacks the most recent PG state,\nit retrieves the log of recent PG changes (or a complete\ncontent summary, if needed) from current or prior OSDs\nin the PG in order to determine the correct (most recent)\nPG contents. The primary then sends each replica an in-\ncremental log update (or complete content summary, if\nneeded), such that all parties know what the PG contents\nOSDI ’06: 7th USENIX Symposium on Operating Systems Design and Implementation\nUSENIX Association\n313\n\n\n=== PDF page 8 ===\nshould be, even if their locally stored object set may not\nmatch. Only after the primary determines the correct PG\nstate and shares it with any replicas is I/O to objects in\nthe PG permitted. OSDs are then independently respon-\nsible for retrieving missing or outdated objects from their\npeers. If an OSD receives a request for a stale or missing\nobject, it delays processing and moves that object to the\nfront of the recovery queue.\nFor example, suppose osd1 crashes and is marked\ndown, and osd2 takes over as primary for pgA. If osd1\nrecovers, it will request the latest map on boot, and a\nmonitor will mark it as up. When osd2 receives the re-\nsulting map update, it will realize it is no longer primary\nfor pgA and send the pgA version number to osd1.\nosd1 will retrieve recent pgA log entries from osd2,\ntell osd2 its contents are current, and then begin pro-\ncessing requests while any updated objects are recovered\nin the background.\nBecause failure recovery is driven entirely by individ-\nual OSDs, each PG affected by a failed OSD will re-\ncover in parallel to (very likely) different replacement\nOSDs. This approach, based on the Fast Recovery Mech-\nanism (FaRM) [37], decreases recovery times and im-\nproves overall data safety.\n5.6\nObject Storage with EBOFS\nAlthough a variety of distributed ﬁle systems use local\nﬁle systems like ext3 to manage low-level storage [4, 12],\nwe found their interface and performance to be poorly\nsuited for object workloads [27]. The existing kernel in-\nterface limits our ability to understand when object up-\ndates are safely committed on disk. Synchronous writes\nor journaling provide the desired safety, but only with\na heavy latency and performance penalty. More impor-\ntantly, the POSIX interface fails to support atomic data\nand metadata (e. g., attribute) update transactions, which\nare important for maintaining RADOS consistency.\nInstead, each Ceph OSD manages its local object stor-\nage with EBOFS, an Extent and B-tree based Object File\nSystem. Implementing EBOFS entirely in user space and\ninteracting directly with a raw block device allows us\nto deﬁne our own low-level object storage interface and\nupdate semantics, which separate update serialization\n(for synchronization) from on-disk commits (for safety).\nEBOFS supports atomic transactions (e. g., writes and at-\ntribute updates on multiple objects), and update functions\nreturn when the in-memory caches are updated, while\nproviding asynchronous notiﬁcation of commits.\nA user space approach, aside from providing greater\nﬂexibility and easier implementation, also avoids cum-\nbersome interaction with the Linux VFS and page cache,\nboth of which were designed for a different interface and\nworkload. While most kernel ﬁle systems lazily ﬂush\nupdates to disk after some time interval, EBOFS aggres-\nsively schedules disk writes, and opts instead to cancel\npending I/O operations when subsequent updates ren-\nder them superﬂuous. This provides our low-level disk\nscheduler with longer I/O queues and a corresponding\nincrease in scheduling efﬁciency. A user-space sched-\nuler also makes it easier to eventually prioritize work-\nloads (e. g., client I/O versus recovery) or provide quality\nof service guarantees [36].\nCentral to the EBOFS design is a robust, ﬂexible, and\nfully integrated B-tree service that is used to locate ob-\njects on disk, manage block allocation, and index collec-\ntions (placement groups). Block allocation is conducted\nin terms of extents—start and length pairs—instead of\nblock lists, keeping metadata compact. Free block ex-\ntents on disk are binned by size and sorted by location,\nallowing EBOFS to quickly locate free space near the\nwrite position or related data on disk, while also limit-\ning long-term fragmentation. With the exception of per-\nobject block allocation information, all metadata is kept\nin memory for performance and simplicity (it is quite\nsmall, even for large volumes). Finally, EBOFS aggres-\nsively performs copy-on-write: with the exception of su-\nperblock updates, data is always written to unallocated\nregions of disk.\n6\nPerformance and Scalability Evaluation\nWe evaluate our prototype under a range of microbench-\nmarks to demonstrate its performance, reliability, and\nscalability. In all tests, clients, OSDs, and MDSs are\nuser processes running on a dual-processor Linux clus-\nter with SCSI disks and communicating using TCP. In\ngeneral, each OSD or MDS runs on its own host, while\ntens or hundreds of client instances may share the same\nhost while generating workload.\n6.1\nData Performance\nEBOFS provides superior performance and safety se-\nmantics, while the balanced distribution of data gener-\nated by CRUSH and the delegation of replication and\nfailure recovery allow aggregate I/O performance to\nscale with the size of the OSD cluster.\n6.1.1\nOSD Throughput\nWe begin by measuring the I/O performance of a 14-node\ncluster of OSDs. Performance was\nmeasured using a fresh ﬁle system. Experience with an\nearlier EBOFS design suggests it will experience signiﬁ-\ncantly lower fragmentation than ext3, but we have not yet\nevaluated the current implementation on an aged ﬁle sys-\ntem. In any case, we expect the performance of EBOFS\nafter aging to be no worse than the others.\n6.1.2\nWrite Latency\nFigure 7 shows the synchronous write latency (y) for a\nsingle writer with varying write sizes (x) and replica-\nWrite Size (KB)\n4\n16\n64\n256\n1024\nWrite Latency (ms)\n0\n5\n10\n15\n20\nno replication\n2x replication\n3x replication\nsync write\nsync lock, async write\nFigure 7: Write latency for varying write sizes and repli-\ncation. Because the primary OSD simultaneously retrans-\nmits updates to all replicas, small writes incur a mini-\nmal latency increase for more than two replicas.\nFor\nlarger writes, the cost of retransmission dominates; 1 MB\nwrites (not shown) take 13 ms for one replica, and 2.5\ntimes longer (33ms) for three.\nCeph clients partially\nmask this latency for synchronous writes over 128 KB\nby acquiring exclusive locks and then asynchronously\nﬂushing the data to disk.\nAlternatively, write-sharing\napplications can opt to use O LAZY. With consistency\nthus relaxed, clients can buffer small writes and submit\nonly large, asynchronous writes to OSDs; the only la-\ntency seen by applications will be due to clients which\nﬁll their caches waiting for data to ﬂush to disk.\n6.1.3\nData Distribution and Scalability\nCeph’s data performance scales nearly linearly in the\nnumber of OSDs.\nCRUSH distributes data pseudo-\nrandomly such that OSD utilizations can be accurately\nmodeled by a binomial or normal distribution—what one\nexpects from a perfectly random process [29].\nVari-\nOSDI ’06: 7th USENIX Symposium on Operating Systems Design and Implementation\nUSENIX Association\n315\n\n\n=== PDF page 10 ===\nance in utilizations decreases as the number of groups\nincreases: for 100 placement groups per OSD the stan-\ndard deviation is 10%; for 1000 groups it is 3%. Fig-\nure 8 shows per-OSD write throughput as the cluster\nscales using CRUSH, a simple hash function, and a linear\nstriping strategy to distribute data in 4096 or 32768 PGs\namong available OSDs. Linear striping balances load\nperfectly for maximum throughput to provide a bench-\nmark for comparison, but like a simple hash function,\nit fails to cope with device failures or other OSD clus-\nter changes. Because data placement with CRUSH or\na hash is stochastic, throughputs are lower with fewer\nPGs: greater variance in OSD utilizations causes request\nqueue lengths to drift apart under our entangled client\nworkload.\nBecause devices can become overﬁlled or\noverutilized with small probability, dragging down per-\nformance, CRUSH can correct such situations by of-\nﬂoading any fraction of the allocation for OSDs specially\nmarked in the cluster map. Unlike the hash and linear\nstrategies, CRUSH also minimizes data migration under\ncluster expansion while maintaining a balanced distribu-\ntion. CRUSH calculations are O(log n) (for a cluster of\nn OSDs) and take only tens of microseconds, allowing\nclusters to grow to hundreds of thousands of OSDs.\n6.2\nMetadata Performance\nCeph’s MDS cluster offers enhanced POSIX semantics\nwith excellent scalability. We measure performance via\na partial workload lacking any data I/O; OSDs in these\nexperiments are used solely for metadata storage.\n6.2.1\nMetadata Update Latency\nWe ﬁrst consider the latency associated with metadata\nupdates (e. g., mknod or mkdir). A single client creates\na series of ﬁles and directories which the MDS must\nsynchronously journal to a cluster of OSDs for safety.\nWe consider both a diskless MDS, where all metadata is\nstored in a shared OSD cluster, and one which also has\na local disk serving as the primary OSD for its journal.\nFigure 9(a) shows the latency (y) associated with meta-\ndata updates in both cases with varying metadata repli-\ncation (x) (where zero corresponds to no journaling at\nall). Journal entries are ﬁrst written to the primary OSD\nand then replicated to any additional OSDs. With a local\ndisk, the initial hop from the MDS to the (local) primary\nOSD takes minimal time, allowing update latencies for\n2× replication similar to 1× in the diskless model. In\nboth cases, more than two replicas incurs little additional\nlatency because replicas update in parallel.\n6.2.2\nMetadata Read Latency\nThe behavior of metadata reads (e. g., readdir, stat, open)\nis more complex. Figure 9(b) shows cumulative time (y)\nconsumed by a client walking 10,000 nested directories\nwith a readdir in each directory and a stat on each ﬁle. Subsequent MDS interaction can\nbe eliminated by using readdirplus, which explicitly bun-\ndles stat and readdir results in a single operation, or by\nrelaxing POSIX to allow stats immediately following a\nreaddir to be served from client caches (the default).\n6.2.3\nMetadata Scaling\nWe evaluate metadata scalability using a 430 node par-\ntition of the alc Linux cluster at Lawrence Livermore\nNational Laboratory (LLNL).
